This Order was instituted by <term>Godfrey of Bouillon</term> <note>A leader of the First Crusade and the first ruler of the Kingdom of Jerusalem (c. 1060–1100). The date 1330 mentioned here is anachronistic, typical of legendary Masonic histories.</note>, in Palestine, in 1330, after the decline of the Christian Armies, and was only shared with French Masons some time later, and to a very small number, in reward for the obliging services they rendered to several of our English and Scottish Knights, from whom <term>true Masonry</term> is derived. Their Mother Lodge <note>original: 'Métropole-Loge'</note> is situated on the mountain of <term>Heredom</term> <note>original: 'Héré-don'; a mythical mountain in Scotland often cited in the high degrees of Freemasonry as the site of the first or highest lodge.</note>, where the first Lodge was held

the right, a small Altar shall be placed, covered with a carpet, which will hold three yellow wax candles, arranged in a square <term>en équerre: placed to form a right angle, symbolizing virtue and rectitude</term>, & the Book of Wisdom. The Lodge must be illuminated by nine yellow candles, suspended in a chandelier, or placed indiscriminately in candlesticks on the floor; but one must be separated from the others by at least one foot. If one wishes to illuminate it further, one may represent the Burning Bush <note>A reference to the biblical account in Exodus 3:2; here it symbolizes divine light and the presence of the Deity.</note> with leaves and tree branches which shall be lit by small lamps. The Lodge [design] must be sketched in chalk upon the floor of the room, as it is represented hereafter <note>This refers to the "tracing board," a floor drawing that depicts the symbols of the degree for the candidate to study.</note>. All around shall be seats upon which the Brothers sit. This Lodge being the Council of Nine, it absolutely cannot be held unless there are nine [members], of whom the first two are Solomon & Hiram, King of Tyre, both of whom occupy the Throne; but Solomon presides on the right. These two Kings must wear crowns upon their heads. That of Solomon is enriched with precious stones; there shall be none on that of the King of Tyre; he will only carry a blue scepter gilded with threads, & surmounted by a luminous triangle, emblem of Wisdom & perfection. ## ON FREEMASONRY.

hand a large dagger. The rest of the clothing will be similar to that of the Brothers, except that Solomon will have gloves adorned with a fringe, and the **apron** <note>original: "tablier"</note> bordered with silver lace. All the Brothers will be dressed in black, and will wear a small **plastron** <term>plastron: an ornamental chest piece or breastplate worn over the heart</term> on the left side, on which will be embroidered a **death’s head** <term>death’s head: a skull, used in these degrees to represent mortality and the mourning for the murdered Master, Hiram Abiff</term> with a bone and a dagger **in saltire** <term>in saltire: crossed in the shape of an X, a common heraldic and Masonic arrangement</term>, in silver, the whole surrounded by the motto: *Conquer or die.* <note>original: "Vaincre ou mourir"</note> They will have a large black-moiré **cordon** <term>cordon: a wide ceremonial sash worn across the torso from shoulder to hip</term>, four fingers wide, hanging from right to left, bearing on the front this motto: *Conquer or die*, embroidered in silver. At the bottom of the sash, there must be a rosette of white ribbon, at the end of which hangs a small dagger in its scabbard. The apron must be of white skin, lined with black; on the **bib** <note>the triangular top flap of the apron</note> shall be embroidered a death’s head with a bone and a sword in saltire, placed beneath a **square** <term>square: one of the primary "working tools" of Masonry, representing morality and virtue</term> embroidered in gold. On the pocket of the apron shall be one large tear, and at the bottom and on the sides eight other smaller tears; <note>In the "Elect" degrees, these tears symbolize the grief of the craft over the loss of their master.</note> at the end of the pocket, a branch of **acacia**. <term>acacia: a sacred wood in the Bible (used for the Ark of the Covenant) and a Masonic symbol of the immortality of the soul</term> The gloves will be lined with black taffeta and bordered with the same.

# FIRST GRADE

### TITLE.

The Master is named **Very Wise** <note>original: "très-Sage"; this role represents King Solomon</note>, the King of Tyre **Very Powerful** <note>Hiram, the King of Tyre, who historically provided cedar and gold for the Temple</note>, & the Brothers **Very Respectable**. There is no Warden <note>In standard Freemasonry, Wardens usually assist the Master; their absence here marks this as a specific "High Degree" or "Elect" ritual</note>; but the **Very Wise**, as soon as he has ascended the Throne, appoints a Brother, who is called the **Intimate of the Council** <term>Intimate of the Council: a specialized officer role, often acting as a confidential secretary or high-ranking assistant to the Master</term>.

## *T I T L E.*

The Master is named Very-Wise <note>original: "très-Sage"; the title for the presiding officer representing King Solomon</note>, the King of Tyre Very-Powerful, & the Brothers Most Respectable. There is no Warden; but the Very-Wise, as soon as he has ascended the Throne, names a Brother, who is called the Intimate of the Council.

### PREPARATION OF THE RECIPIENT.

The Senior Brother, upon arriving, seizes his sword, takes it from him, and sends it to the Lodge by a Brother whom he has taken care to bring with him. This Brother, in presenting it to the Most Wise <note>original: "très-Sage"; a ritual title for Solomon as the presiding officer</note>, says to him, *he is disarmed*. The Senior Brother leads him to the end of the Lodge; he passes a red cord or ribbon over his neck, with which his hands are tied, and in which his body is enclosed. Afterward, his shoes are completely removed, and he is blindfolded <note>original: "bandeau"; a blindfold used in Masonic initiations to represent a state of symbolic darkness.</note>

### FIRST DEGREE

...very thick over his eyes, and bloody gloves in his hands, having his hat removed, and his apron put on in the manner of a Mason <note>A specific way of wearing the ritual apron, which varies by degree.</note>. You may, however, hope for indulgence, if sincerity guides your words. If you are innocent, follow me with confidence.” Next, the Senior Brother places his dagger against the heart of the Candidate <note>original: "Récipiendaire"; the person receiving the initiation</note> and leads him to the door of the Lodge; he must have the key; he opens it, introduces the Candidate, and places him in the West <note>original: "l'Occident"; symbolic of the place where the day ends and where candidates often begin their ritual journey toward the light.</note>. When he is there, and all the Brothers are seated, Solomon says to the Candidate: *What do you seek?* The Senior Brother dictates his answers: *The reward that is due to me.*

*Q.* Do you believe that Masons authorize crime and murder? Tremble rather at the just punishment reserved for you! Who are you?

*A.* The best of Masons, the most zealous of all the Brothers, or at least the most worthy of this title.

# ON FREEMASONRY.

...you present yourself in this sacred place, your hands stained with a blood that is surely innocent? Everything testifies against you, everything proclaims the murder.
*A.* I submit to everything, if I am guilty.
The King of Tyre <note>Hiram, the King of Tyre, who according to biblical and Masonic tradition provided materials and craftsmen for Solomon's Temple.</note> says: *Hiram Abif* be avenged.
All the Brothers say N. N. M. <note>original: "N. N. M."; these initials in this context typically refer to "Nekum" or "Nakam," a Hebrew word for "vengeance," which is a central theme in the "Elect" degrees of Masonry.</note>
My Brothers, be glad, the murderer of Hiram is discovered.
*Q.* The deception is too crude; he is already guilty, since he seeks to deceive us; what do you answer?
*A.* That it is wrong to suspect me of the murder of a Master <note>Refers to Hiram Abif, the legendary master builder of Solomon's Temple, whose death is the central myth of Masonic ritual.</note> whose memory I respect; I come only with the intent of giving you news of him, through the discoveries I have made.
*Q.* What then is this news?
*A.* A cave, a burning bush, a gushing fountain, and a dog for a guide have shown me the place of his retreat <note>This describes the symbolic journey to find the assassin who fled after killing the Master. The dog is a common symbol of fidelity leading the way to truth.</note>.
*Q.* What are the guarantees of this?
*A.* My hands, drenched in the blood of three animals—the lion, the tiger, and the bear—which he had tamed to guard the entrance to his cave, and which I destroyed to reach it <note>These three animals often represent the passions or vices that a Mason must overcome to reach the inner "cave" of self-knowledge or truth.</note>.

<summary>This page details a ritual interrogation where a Candidate, accused of murder, defends himself by claiming he has found the hideout of the true assassin. He describes a symbolic journey involving a guiding dog and the slaying of three guardian animals to reach a hidden cave.</summary>
<keywords>Hiram Abif, King of Tyre, Elect degrees, Masonic ritual, ritual vengeance, symbolic cave, guarding animals, Solomon's Temple, initiation ceremony</keywords>

### FIRST GRADE

*Q.* What do you come to ask?
*A.* To throw myself at the King’s feet to take his orders, and to see if he wishes me to deliver Abiram <note>Abiram (often associated with the biblical Abiram, but here representing one of the three "unworthy craftsmen" or "ruffians") is the legendary assassin of the Master Architect, Hiram Abif.</note> to him, dead or alive.
*Q.* What proof do you give of your faith?
*A.* The most sacred promises shall be the guarantees of my innocence; and I consent to be exposed to the most rigorous punishments if I am found to be a criminal.

Intimate Brother, since this Brother begins to calm our suspicions, have him advance by nine steps—three of the Apprentice, three of the Fellow Craft <note>original: "Compagnon"; the second level of Freemasonry.</note>, and three of the Master—up to the Throne, to come and swear his obligation into our hands.

The Intimate Brother has the Candidate <note>original: "Récipiendaire"; the person receiving the degree.</note> advance as ordered, up to the Throne, where, having arrived, he places his right knee on the ground, his naked right hand on the Book of Wisdom, while holding in his left the compasses which embrace a mallet. Solomon places his dagger on the candidate’s forehead, and the Intimate Brother places a naked sword on his back; then the Most Wise <note>original: "très-Sage"; the ritual title for the presiding officer representing King Solomon.</note>, striking a blow with his scepter on the Altar so that all the Brothers rise, says to him: “Take heed of what you are about to do. ### O B L I G A T I O N.

> „ I promise, on my word as an honest man, and before this august Assembly, at the foot of the highest Power of Masonry, never to reveal to any man who has not done what I have done, the secrets that lead to and bestow the sublime title of <term>Elect Master</term> <note>original: "Maître Elu"; a high degree in the Masonic rite centered on the legend of the pursuit and punishment of the assassins of the Master Architect, Hiram Abif.</note>. I promise to scrupulously fulfill its obligations at the peril of my blood, in whatever circumstance it may be, to sacrifice to the spirit <note>original: "mânes"; refers to the shades or spirits of the dead in Roman mythology, here applied to the legendary Hiram.</note> of Hiram those perjurers who might reveal any of our secrets to the <term>Profane</term> <note>original: "Profanes"; the standard Masonic term for those who have not been initiated into the order.</note>. I shall keep my engagements; otherwise, let the most hideous death be the atonement for my perjury: after my eyes have been deprived of light by a red-hot iron, may my body become the prey of vultures, and may my memory be held in execration by the <term>children of the widow</term> <note>original: "enfants de la veuve"; a poetic and traditional title for Freemasons, referencing the biblical description of Hiram Abif as the son of a widow from the tribe of Naphtali.</note> throughout the whole earth. So mote it be <note>original: "Ainsi soit-il"; the traditional concluding phrase for Masonic oaths.</note>.

# FIRST GRADE

We have, in this grade, as in all the others, a sign, a word, and a <term>touch</term><note>Also known as a "grip," this is a secret handshake used to verify a member's rank.</note>.

The sign is made by the one who requests it, by drawing his <term>dagger</term><note>original: "poignard"; a key symbol in the 'Elect' degrees representing the pursuit of the assassins of Hiram Abiff.</note> with the right hand, and raising it as if to strike the forehead.

The person who responds closes their right hand; and with the fist, thus closed, raises it, and then turns it over.

The touch, for the one who initiates it, consists of—after having closed the right hand—raising the thumb and presenting it to the person being tested. He must respond by seizing the thumb with his whole hand, using the same hand; then the Requester repeats the same thing, and the Responder does it once more.

The word is N. N. M. <note>These initials typically represent the Hebrew phrase "Nekum Nakah," meaning "vengeance is taken," central to the theme of this degree.</note>

# FIRST DEGREE

---

## CATECHISM.

*Q.* Are you an <term>Elected Master</term><note>original: "Maître Elu"; the ninth degree in the Adonhiramite rite. This degree focuses on the legend of the nine masters chosen by King Solomon to find the assassins of Hiram Abiff.</note>?
*A.* Yes, <term>Most Wise</term><note>original: "très-Sage"; the title used for the presiding officer of the Lodge in this particular degree.</note>, I am.
*Q.* By what shall I know it?
*A.* By the sign, the <term>token</term><note>original: "attouchement"; a specific secret handshake used as a means of recognition between members of the same rank.</note>, and the word.
*Q.* Give them.
*A. *He gives them.*
*Q.* Where were you received as an Elected Master?
*A.* In the Hall of Solomon.
*Q.* What motive led you to seek this title?
*A.* The desire to avenge the death of Hiram.
*Q.* Who was the murderer of Hiram?
*A.* <term>Abiram</term><note>In this Masonic tradition, Abiram is identified as the chief assassin of Hiram Abiff. While the name appears in the Bible, here it is used symbolically within the ritual's drama.</note>, whose name signifies murderer or assassin.
*Q.* By what way did you arrive at the place of vengeance?
*A.* By dark and unknown paths.
*Q.* Who led you there?
*A.* A stranger.

<vocab>Elected Master, Catechism, Solomon, Hiram, Abiram, vengeance</vocab>

<summary>This page presents the opening of the Masonic catechism for the Elected Master degree, detailing the symbolic pursuit of Hiram's murderer. The initiate answers questions that establish his new rank and his role in the allegorical quest for justice.</summary>
<keywords>Elected Master, ritual, catechism, Hiram Abiff, Abiram, Solomon's Hall, Masonic recognition, vengeance</keywords>

--- Page 58 ---
<meta>This page continues the catechism for the "Elected Master" degree (specifically the Elect of Nine). While the previous page established the candidate’s motive—to avenge the death of the master architect Hiram—this page details the symbolic climax of that quest: the discovery and execution of the assassin in a secret cavern.</meta>

### ON MASONRY.

Q. Where was the place of vengeance located?
A. At the foot of a <term>burning bush</term><note>A symbol likely borrowed from the story of Moses, representing a place of divine revelation or a turning point in the spiritual journey.</note>, inside a dark cavern.
Q. What did you find in this cavern?
A. The traitor <term>Abiram</term><note>The name given to the primary antagonist in this Masonic degree. He represents the vice of treachery.</note>, a light, a spring, and a dagger.
Q. What was the use of all these?
A. The light illuminated my way, the spring quenched my thirst, and the dagger was reserved to avenge the death of Hiram by the blow dealt to Abiram, which caused him to fall dead on the spot.
Q. Did this wretched man say any word?
A. Yes, a word, but one that I cannot utter aloud.
Q. Give me the first letter; I will tell you the last.
A. <term>Vengeance, Struck</term><note>original: "Nekar, nekam." These are Hebrew-derived words. *Nakam* (נקם) translates to "vengeance," while *Nekar* is traditionally interpreted in this ritual context as "the stranger" or "is struck."</note>.
Q. What did you do with Abiram's body?
A. I cut off his head, which I carried to Solomon, to inform him that the vengeance was accomplished.
Q. What time was it when you arrived?
A. The break of dawn.

# FIRST DEGREE

*Q.* How many <term>Elected Masters</term><note>A Masonic rank focusing on the theme of justice and the symbolic punishment of those who betrayed the master architect Hiram Abiff.</note> were there for this vengeance?
*A. Nine.*
*Q.* What remains for you to do?
*A. Nothing, since all is accomplished.*
*Q.* What time is it?
*A. The onset of night, the hour at which I left the cavern.*
*Q.* What is the password?
*A. <term>Sterkin</term><note>A traditional password for this degree. Its meaning is debated by scholars, sometimes linked to the Hebrew word for "foulness" or "decay," referring to the state of the traitor's body or the discovery of the crime.</note>*

My Brothers, may such a memorable hour always be present in our minds, and constantly remind us of the zeal of the nine Masters so that we may imitate them.

<term>Solomon</term><note>The presiding officer of the ceremony, representing the biblical King Solomon.</note> strikes seven equal blows on the <term>Altar</term>, and <term>Hiram</term><note>Representing Hiram, King of Tyre, who assisted Solomon in the building of the Temple.</note> strikes the last two rapidly, after which the <term>Most Wise</term><note>A title given to the master presiding over the Lodge in this specific degree.</note> says: "My Brothers, the vengeance is accomplished; the Council may withdraw, the Lodge of Elected Masters is closed."
All the Brothers strike nine times with their hands; seven equal, two rapid, and the acclamations are made.

# CONCERNING MASONRY.

...before having it kissed by all the Brothers, each one in particular, before placing it around the neck.

The <term>Elected Masters</term><note>A high-ranking Masonic degree where members symbolize the "Elect" chosen to seek justice for the murder of the master builder, Hiram Abiff.</note> must never be present in any Lodge, whether inferior or superior, without wearing their black <term>sash</term><note>original: "cordon." A ceremonial ribbon worn across the chest, which in this degree is black to symbolize mourning and solemnity.</note> and their dagger, although they must only make use of the latter during Lodges of the Elect.

---

## TABLE LODGE.

THE <term>Table Lodge</term><note>A formal Masonic banquet where the meal is conducted with the same ritual precision as a standard meeting, including specific terminology for the food and drink.</note> is held like the others, with the exception that there is no <term>Warden</term>; but the <term>Senior Brother</term>, placed opposite the two Kings, performs that function.

The Lodge is opened with nine knocks, then several questions from the <term>Catechism</term><note>A series of ritual questions and answers used to test a member's knowledge of the degree's symbols and secrets.</note> are asked, and it is announced that the Lodge of the Elect is open. When <term>toasts</term><note>original: "santés."</note> are proposed, the Brothers draw their daggers and place them across their <term>canons</term><note>original: "canons." In Masonic banquet language, the glasses are referred to as "cannons," and the act of drinking is often called "firing."</note>; the dagger is then removed and placed beside the canon. The toast is drunk in the usual manner, and the dagger is immediately replaced upon the canon in three movements, without making a noise. He who commands the toast makes the sign of request, and all the Brothers make the sign of response, just as when one

# FIRST GRADE, &c.

...wishes to ask for the floor. Knives are called <term>daggers</term><note>original: "poignards." In the specialized "banquet language" of a Table Lodge, everyday objects are given ritual names. Since this degree focuses on the pursuit of Hiram's assassins, the knives are appropriately called daggers.</note>.

When a <term>Lodge of the Elect</term><note>Also known as the "Elect of Nine," this is a "higher" or "side" degree that follows the three foundational degrees of Craft Masonry.</note> is held and there is an initiation, the proceedings begin by opening in the <term>Master's</term><note>Refers to the Third Degree (Master Mason). High degree rituals often require the assembly to first convene in a lower degree before "elevating" the Lodge to a higher one.</note> degree. The <term>Candidate</term><note>original: "Récipiendaire." The person being admitted into the degree.</note> is present. When the Lodge is open, the <term>Worshipful Master</term><note>original: "Respectable." The traditional title for the presiding officer of the Lodge.</note> has the Candidate sit at the foot of the Altar. All the Brothers sit on seats brought in behind them; then the Worshipful Master exhorts them to lend an attentive ear to the discourse about to be delivered to them, which concerns their initiation. When the discourse is finished, the Candidates are made to stand and are sent to the <term>Chamber of Reflection</term><note>original: "chambre de réflexion." A darkened room, often decorated with symbols of mortality like skulls and hourglasses, where the candidate meditates on their life and intentions before the ceremony begins.</note>; the Master’s Lodge is closed, and the Lodge of the Elect is opened, as is described at the beginning of this <term>Treatise</term><note>original: "Traité." Referring to the ritual manual itself.</note>.

### O B L I G A T I O N.

„ I swear and promise, on my word of honor, on the faith of an honest man, in the presence of the **Grand Architect of the Universe** <note>original: "Grand Architecte de l'Univers." A deistic term used in Masonry to refer to God, allowing men of different faiths to join together.</note>, and before this Assembly, to keep and observe the mysteries of the second **Grade of Elect** which is about to be entrusted to me, not only towards <term>Profanes</term><note>original: "Profanes." Those who are not initiated into the secrets of Freemasonry.</note>, but also towards the Brothers who are in grades inferior to this one; all under the penalties set forth by my first obligation; consenting further to have my tongue torn out, and to be deemed infamous <note>original: "un infame." To be stripped of one's reputation and social standing—a punishment often considered worse than death in the 18th century.</note>, from which may God preserve me, and be my aid. *Amen.*

<header>OF FREEMASONRY.</header>

## CATECHISM.

*Q.* <note>original: "D." for "Demande" (Question).</note> **Do you know** of any other Elect besides the one of the letters N. N. M.? <note>The letters N.N.M. typically refer to the "First Elect" degree and stand for the Hebrew words *Nekam* (Vengeance), *Nekamah* (it is accomplished), and *Maccabee* (the name of the first Elect), though interpretations varied across 18th-century rites.</note>
*A.* <note>original: "R." for "Réponse" (Answer).</note> Yes, I know the letter P.
*Q.* What does this letter signify?
*A.* It is the initial of the name of the **Unknown** <note>In Masonic legend, a stranger—often described as a shepherd—informed King Solomon of the location where the assassin of the master builder Hiram was hiding.</note> who came to discover to Solomon the hiding place of **Abiram**. <note>The name given to one of the three ruffians who murdered Hiram Abiff. In other versions of the legend, the names Akirop or Jubelum are used.</note>
*Q.* Pronounce his full name.
*A.* **Pérignan**, after whom this grade is named. <note>The Grade of the "Elect of Pérignan" focuses on the pursuit and capture of the traitors. Pérignan is the legendary name given to the shepherd who guided the nine Elect to the cavern.</note>
*Q.* How were you introduced into the Lodge?
*A.* By twenty-seven knocks struck at different times, in groups of nine.
*Q.* What do these repeated knocks signify?
<insert>LIBRARY OF ST. LOUIS, NIJMEGEN</insert> <note>This is a library stamp from the original physical manuscript.</note>
*A.* Three things: first, that I was one of the nine **Elect** <term>Elect</term><note>From the French *Élu*. These degrees represent a specialized class of Masons tasked with maintaining justice and honoring the memory of the Master.</note> who went in search of the assassin, or at least that I desired to be one of them. Second, the nine Masters who went in search of the body of our dear **Master Hiram**. <note>Hiram Abiff, the chief architect of King Solomon's Temple, whose death is the central allegory of Freemasonry.</note> Third, the blows that were struck at the three gates by the three **false Brothers**. <note>Refers to the three ruffians who attacked Hiram at the East, South, and West gates of the Temple when he refused to reveal the secrets of a Master Mason.</note>

### SECOND GRADE

*Q.* <note>original: "D." for "Demande" (Question).</note> What do these three letters **R. G. A.** signify, which appear in the tracing board of the Lodge?
*A.* <note>original: "R." for "Réponse" (Answer).</note> The names of the assassins of the master **Hiram**.
*Q.* Name them.
*A.* **Romvel**, **Gravelot**, **Abiram**. <note>In traditional English Freemasonry, these figures are usually named Jubela, Jubelo, and Jubelum. The names used here—Romvel (sometimes polemically linked to Cromwell in early French Masonic legends), Gravelot, and Abiram—are characteristic of 18th-century French "Elect" degrees.</note>
*Q.* How did these three wretches position themselves to execute their detestable crime?
*A.* Romvel at the West gate, armed with a **rule**; Gravelot at the North gate, armed with a **mallet**; and Abiram at the South gate, armed with a **lever**. It was he who struck him to the ground and left him dead.
*Q.* What was the fate of Romvel and Gravelot? Our **First Elect** <term>First Elect: Refers to the previous degree in this rite, the "Elect of Nine," which focuses primarily on the capture and execution of Abiram, the first of the three assassins found.</term> only teaches us about that of Abiram.
*A.* Solomon discovered that they had perished miserably in the **Land of Cabul**, where they had fled. <note>original: "Pays Cabule." In the Bible (1 Kings 9:13), the "Land of Cabul" refers to a region given to Hiram of Tyre by Solomon; in this Masonic legend, it serves as the site where the remaining conspirators met their end.</note>
*Q.* How did **Pérignan**, that unknown man from the first grade, know that Abiram had taken refuge in his cavern?
*A.* Pérignan, working near a bush at the foot of which the cave was located, heard his dog begin to bark; he looked and saw a man enter, looking completely terrified. His curiosity led him to find out who it was. Abiram, <meta>catchword: seeing</meta>

<summary>This page of the catechism identifies the three assassins of Hiram Abiff as Romvel, Gravelot, and Abiram, describing their weapons and the locations of their attack. It further explains that while Abiram was found in a cave by the shepherd Pérignan, the other two fled to the Land of Cabul where they died.</summary>

<keywords>Romvel, Gravelot, Abiram, Hiram Abiff, Land of Cabul, Pérignan, Elect of Pérignan, Masonic assassins, tracing board, Solomon</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the "Catechism" for the Second Grade of the Elect (specifically the Elect of Pérignan). The narrative focus remains on the capture of Abiram, the chief assassin of Hiram Abiff, and the role of the character Pérignan (here also referred to as the "Stranger") in his discovery.</meta>

<page-num>33</page-num>

<header>ON FREEMASONRY.</header>

...seeing himself discovered by this **Stranger**, he threw himself at his feet, entrusted him with his secret, begging him not to reveal it to **Solomon**; he kissed his hands to move him to pity, and begged him to help him in the pressing hunger that was devouring him.

*Q.* <note>original: "D." for "Demande" (Question).</note> Why did the **Stranger** inform **Solomon**?

*A.* <note>original: "R." for "Réponse" (Answer).</note> To satisfy the Edict issued by this wise King, and to obtain the reward for it.

*Q.* For how long did **Pérignan** feed **Abiram** before discovering <note>In this context, "discovering" means revealing his location to the authorities.</note> him to **Solomon**?

*A.* Seven whole days.

*Q.* Why did he delay so long in declaring it?

*A.* Because he only learned of the King's Edict seven days after the discovery, while going to the City to seek food for himself and for **Abiram**.

*Q.* What is the sacred word of this grade?

*A.* **Moabon**. <note>The word "Moabon" is used in several Masonic degrees. Its meaning here—referring to the punishment of a crime—differs from the interpretations found in the Master Mason or other Elect degrees, where it is often associated with the "son of the father" or the state of Hiram's body.</note>

*Q.* What does this word signify?

*A.* Praised be God that the crime and the criminal are punished.

*Q.* What is the sign of this grade?

*A.* It is to make a gesture as if tearing out one’s tongue, while extending the hands. <note>Such signs, often called "penal signs," symbolically represent the fate of those who betray secrets or commit heinous crimes.</note>

*Q.* What is the response to this sign?

*A.* It is to raise the hands and the eyes [toward heaven].

# SECOND GRADE

...toward Heaven, as if to implore mercy.

*Q.* <note>original: "D." for "Demande" (Question).</note> From where do you derive this sign?

*A.* <note>original: "R." for "Réponse" (Answer).</note> Partly from my obligation <note>The formal oath or promise of secrecy and conduct made during the initiation.</note>, and partly from the shock experienced by **Abiram** upon seeing himself discovered.

*Q.* What is your token <note>original: "attouchement"; a secret handshake or physical grip used to identify fellow members.</note>?

*A.* To offer the hand to the one you wish to recognize, who must then take it and kiss it.

*Q.* To what does this token relate?

*A.* To the kissing of the hand that **Abiram** gave to **Pérignan** to induce him to remain silent.

*Q.* What is the password?

*A.* **ABIRAM**, which signifies murderer or assassin. <note>While usually a proper name in the Bible, in this ritual context, it is treated as a synonym for the crime of the assassin.</note>

*Q.* What became of the head of this wretch?

*A.* It was embalmed, and **Solomon** had it placed on the tip of a pike with a dagger positioned below it in a saltire <note>Crossed in the shape of an "X".</note>, and displayed at the north <note>original: "septentrion."</note> of the **Temple**, to show that crime does not remain unpunished.

*Q.* What is signified by the nine circuits <note>original: "tours"; referring to the ritual walking around the lodge.</note> that you performed during your journey, before taking your obligation?

*A.* The nine days that **Abiram** remained hidden in the cave.

### D I S C O U R S E.

You have finally reached the grade of **Second Elect**, Worshipful Master <note>The presiding officer of the lodge addressing the initiate.</note>, a grade that has long been the object of your desires, which your zeal and labors have secured for you; allow me to congratulate you on it. We have entrusted it to you wholeheartedly, and all the more willingly, because this favor will undoubtedly make you worthy, through your efforts, of penetrating the sublime mysteries that remain for us to reveal to you.

You learned, in the first grade of **Elect**, the fate of the treacherous **Abiram**; and the second instructs you in the fatal end of the two other villains, **Romwel** and **Gravelot** <note>In this specific Masonic tradition, these are the names given to the other two assassins of the master architect Hiram Abiff. In other rites, they are sometimes called Jubelo and Jubelum.</note>, who, after having long wandered from Province to Province, dragging the remorse of their crime everywhere, perished miserably: the just effect of divine vengeance, which never leaves crime without punishment.

All the allegory contained in this new Grade is very easy to develop: you will find it all laid out in this tracing board <note>original: "tableau"; a symbolic illustration or floor cloth used in the lodge to teach the myths and symbols of the degree.</note> which presents itself to your eyes, and of which the principal and most striking object of horror

to work at the Temple, is to teach

similar fate. All the mysteries of this
Grade of Elect <note>original: "Grade d'Elu"; a specific rank in this Masonic rite focused on the theme of justice.</note> have a moral connection,
just like those of the other Grades that you
already possess. The sign of this one, by
pretending to tear out one's tongue,
teaches us thereby how great
our discretion must be, & the penalty due to those
who are indiscreet. The response to this sign, by lift-
ing the hands & eyes to Heaven,
indicates to us the state of a man surprised &
stunned at the sight of a strik-
ing event, which Providence has arranged to
bring him back to his duty, or to pun-
ish him. The sacred word <note>A password used to identify members of this specific degree.</note> that we pro-

will of the Supreme Being, & the satis-
faction we feel at seeing the cri-
me & the criminal punished.

Furthermore, this Grade, as eminent as it
is, is, so to speak, only prepara-
tory to the third Grade, whose sublimity it heralds,
& whose profound mystery you will
one day know, if your
zeal, your discretion, & your other
good qualities do not falter.

# MANNER
## OF CLOSING THE LODGE.

*Q.* <note>original: "D." for "Demande" (Question)</note> **D**o you know, in your capacity as an Elect, any other letters than those of N. N. M.? <note>These initials typically refer to secret names or passwords associated with this specific Masonic degree.</note>
*A.* <note>original: "R." for "Réponse" (Answer)</note> Yes: I know the letter P.
*Q.* At what hour is the Lodge closed?
*A.* At the break of day.
*Q.* What time is it?
*A.* The day is about to appear.
Since it is day, and our labors are finished, venerable first and second Wardens, <term>Wardens</term> <note>Officers who assist the Master in governing the Lodge; they are usually seated at the West and South.</note> announce upon your columns <note>The "columns" refers to the two rows of members or the specific stations where the Wardens preside.</note> that the Lodge is closed; which they then execute: afterwards, the usual applause and acclamations are made.

...killed in the cave, beneath the burning bush <note>In the preceding "Elect of the Nine" degree, the first assassin (often named Abiram or Akirop) is discovered in a cavern and killed.</note>, was a murderer of Hiram. In Masonic symbolism, the three assassins strike Hiram with different tools: the plumb rule, the square, and the lever (or gavel/setting maul).</note>, who struck down your Master, and whose head Solomon had embalmed, so that it could be preserved and be exposed in public with those of his accomplices, once they had been discovered; this did not take long, since six months later, Ben-Gabel <note>Likely a reference to Ben-Geber, identified in 1 Kings 4:13 as one of King Solomon’s regional administrators.</note>, one of Solomon’s Intendants, through the searches he conducted in the vicinity of the Country of Gath <note>Original: "Geth." In the biblical account, Maacah is associated with the King of Gath.</note>, King of Gath, to request him to deliver these assassins to the trusted persons he was sending to lead them to Jerusalem, there to receive the punishment due to their crime.

->DE LA MAÇONNERIE.<-

### S I G N S.

The first is to close the right hand, the thumb raised as if holding a dagger, bringing it under the chin, and lowering it along the body, as if wishing to cut oneself open. The second, which is the response of the Examiner, is to extend the hand, pretending to cut one's throat with the thumb.

The sacred word is *Zéomet*; & the response is *Eléham*, which will also serve as a password <note>Original: "passage." A word used to gain entry to the meeting space.</note> to enter the Temple.

The first grip <note>Original: "attouchement." A secret handshake or physical touch used for recognition between members.</note> is to give two small taps with the index finger on the joint of the little finger, which alludes to the two traitors discovered <note>Referring to Sterkin and Oterfut, the final two assassins captured in this degree's legend.</note>. The second is to take the right hand of the Examiner with the five fingers of the right hand extended; which signifies three times five, & consequently the number of the fifteen Elect <note>The "Elect of the Fifteen" refers to the fifteen masters chosen by Solomon to find the assassins.</note>.

The sash <note>Original: "cordon." A ceremonial silk ribbon worn over the shoulder as part of the Masonic regalia.</note> of the Grand Master Elect is of black ribbon, of the greatest width, with three ends of poppy-red <note>Original: "ponceau." A deep, brilliant red color.</note> ribbon, after which a death’s head <note>A skull, a common masonic symbol representing mortality and the fate of the traitors.</note> shall be attached: the top of the sash shall be embroidered with fifteen silver tears.

<summary>This page details the ritual recognition signs, sacred words, and ceremonial regalia for the Elect of the Fifteen degree, emphasizing the number fifteen and the themes of justice and mortality.</summary>

opened the body from the chest down to the private parts, and they were left in this manner in the heat of the sun for the space of eight hours. The flies and other insects drank their blood. They uttered such lamentable cries that they moved their executioners to compassion; this forced them to cut off their heads. Their bodies were thrown outside of Jerusalem to be exposed to wild beasts. Solomon then ordered that Hoben's head be brought back <note>Hoben was captured earlier in the narrative than the other two; his head is reunited with the others for this symbolic display.</note>, so that all three would be displayed outside the City on pikes, in the same order as these murderers had positioned themselves in the Temple to assassinate Hiram, in order to set an example for all his subjects, and particularly for the worker Masons. <header>ON FREEMASONRY.</header>

### S I G N S.

The first is to close the right hand, with the thumb raised as if holding a dagger; bring it under the chin and move it down along the body, as if wishing to cut oneself open. The second, which is the response from the Examiner, is to extend the hand, while pretending to cut one's throat with the thumb.

The sacred word is *Zéomet*; and the response is *Elébam*, which will also serve as the password to enter the Temple <note>In many Masonic systems, these words are variations or reversals of names found in earlier degrees, used here to verify the member's rank.</note>.

The first grip <note>Original: "attouchement." A secret handshake or physical touch used for identification.</note> is to give two light taps with the index finger on the knuckle of the pinky finger, which alludes to the two traitors discovered <note>Specifically referring to the characters Sterkin and Oterfut mentioned in the previous narrative.</note>. The second is to take the Examiner’s right hand with the five fingers of the right hand extended; this signifies three times five, and consequently the number of the fifteen Elect.

The sash <note>Original: "cordon." A broad ceremonial ribbon worn over the shoulder.</note> of the Grand Master Elect is made of black ribbon, of the greatest width, with three ends of poppy-red <note>Original: "ponceau." A deep, vibrant red color.</note> ribbon, from which a skull shall be attached: the top of the sash shall be embroidered with fifteen silver tears.

<header>THIRD DEGREE <note>In this specific sequence of "Elect" degrees, the "Third Degree" refers to the Elect of the Fifteen, following the Elect of the Nine.</note></header>

### MANNER OF INTRODUCING THE CANDIDATE.

He must be dressed as an **Elect of the Nine**, and hold two skulls, as has been stated previously. When there is no initiation ceremony <note>Original: "réception." The formal ritual of admitting a new member into the degree.</note>, the Lodge opens as follows, with the exception that when the fifteen lights are illuminated, the Grand Master says: “Help me open the Lodge;” then all the Brothers, standing and with heads uncovered, make the same sign.
*Q.* What time is it?
*A.* Five o'clock in the evening.
*Q.* Why?
*A.* Because it is at this hour that the murderers of Hiram were discovered and seized, to be taken to Jerusalem.
*Q.* Have they been discovered?
*A.* Yes, Most Respectable <note>Original: "très-Respectable." A traditional Masonic title used when addressing the presiding officer of the Lodge.</note>, they have been, and presented to Solomon.

> “My Brothers, says the Grand Master,
> “since these two murderers have been
> “discovered and arrested, let us set
> “about having them punished, and learning
> “who they are, in order to show our
> “zeal to avenge the death of Hiram.

All the Brothers strike <note>Likely referring to clapping hands or striking the floor with a gavel or rod in a rhythmic pattern.</note> three times five.

## INSTRUCTION.

Q. Are you an Elected Grand Master?
A. Yes: my zeal and my labor have earned me this rank, along with the esteem of my Superiors.
Q. Where were you received?
A. In Solomon’s private chamber <note>Original: "cabinet." This refers to a private office or inner room where the King conducted confidential business.</note>.
Q. When did he receive you?
A. When he sent me with my Brothers to search for the last two assassins of Hiram.
Q. So you yourself went on the search?
A. Yes, Most Respectable <note>Original: "très-Respectable." A traditional Masonic title of address for the presiding Master of the Lodge.</note>.
Q. Did you feel great joy when the assassins were punished?
A. The three heads depicted on my sash <note>Original: "cordon." This refers to the decorative fabric band worn over the shoulder, which in this degree would be embroidered with symbolic imagery of the three assassins' heads.</note> are proof of it.
Q. What do these three heads signify?
A. Those of the three assassins of Hiram.
Q. You say that you went in search of two.
A. That is true; but the third had already been punished <note>This refers to the narrative of the preceding degree, the "Elect of the Nine," where the first assassin was discovered and killed in a cave.</note>.
Q. What were the names of the two you led to Jerusalem?

A. They were chained by both hands.

*Q.* On what day did you arrive in Jerusalem?

*A.* One month.

*Q.* How many Masters did Solomon elect for this search?

*Q.* What did Solomon order?

*Q.* By what kind of death were they punished?

*A.* They were tied naked to posts by their feet, arms, and necks. Their bodies were sliced open from the chest down to their private parts.

Q. What is the sacred word of the Grand Master Elect?
A. Zéomet <note>Masonic "sacred words" are often derived from Hebrew or are phonetic ciphers; their exact meanings were often obscured or lost to the practitioners themselves.</note>.
Q. What is the password?
A. Eléham.
Q. What time is it?

> “since the death of our Grand Mas-

The Lodge is closed; one strikes three times five knocks <note>The fifteen knocks (three sets of five) correspond to the "Elect of the Fifteen" degree, symbolizing the fifteen fellow-crafts who were sent to search for the assassins.</note>.

## STYLE OF THE APRON.

It is made of white skin, bordered with a black ribbon. In the middle, there must be a tower embroidered in silver and three rosettes of black ribbon: one at each corner, and one on the flap, which signify the three heads; below the flap shall be placed **H**; below the rosette on the left **O**, and to the right **S** <note>These initials—H, O, and S—refer to the names of the assassins provided on the previous page: Hoben, Oterfut, and Sterkin.</note>.

<header>52 THIRD DEGREE</header>

# CATECHISM
## OF THE PERFECT ELECT.

*Q.* Are you an Elect?
*A.* I am.
*Q.* What is the work of the Perfect Elect?
*A.* To rectify morals <note>This refers to the Masonic goal of self-improvement and the refinement of one's ethical character.</note>.
*Q.* Where do the Perfect Elects travel?
*A.* To the sovereign vault <note>The "vault" is a recurring symbol in higher Masonic degrees, representing a secret, sacred place where lost knowledge or truth is rediscovered.</note>.
*Q.* Through where did you pass?
*A.* Through a long corridor, illuminated by 3, 5, 7, and 9.
*Q.* What does the number 3 signify?
*A.* The three principal columns S. W. B. <note>original: "F. S. B." stands for *Force, Sagesse, Beauté* (Strength, Wisdom, and Beauty). These are the three symbolic pillars that support a Masonic Lodge.</note>, the age of an apprentice <note>In Freemasonry, "age" is a symbolic representation of a member's progress and degree level, rather than chronological years.</note>.
*Q.* What does the number 5 signify?
*A.* The five orders of Architecture: Ionic, Doric, Tuscan, Corinthian, and Composite; the age of a Fellow Craft <note>original: "Compagnon." This is the second degree of Craft Masonry.</note>.
*Q.* What does the number 9 signify?
*A.* The nine Masters; the age of a perfect Mason.

<summary>This page presents a catechism for the "Perfect Elect" degree, detailing the symbolic journey through a corridor and the significance of the numbers 3, 5, and 9 in relation to Masonic degrees and architecture.</summary>
<keywords>Perfect Elect, Catechism, Sovereign Vault, Three Columns, Orders of Architecture, Apprentice, Fellow Craft, Master Mason</keywords>

*Q.* What did you find before the Corridor?
*A.* A Respectable Elect, who asked me for the password.
*Q.* How did you give it to him?
*A.* Chibot <note>original: "Chibot." Likely a variation of "Shibboleth," a password mentioned in the Bible (Judges 12:6) and commonly used in various Masonic degrees.</note>, three times.
*Q.* What does the sacred vault contain?
*A.* The word. <note>In Freemasonry, the "Lost Word" represents divine truth or ancient knowledge that was lost upon the death of the master builder, Hiram Abiff, and which candidates seek to rediscover.</note>
*Q.* What is this word?
*A.* The one that was lost in the ruins of the Temple.
*Q.* To whom was this word first given?
*A.* To Moses, in a burning bush.
*Q.* To whom was it transmitted?
*A.* To the Perfect Elect alone.
*Q.* What was this word used for?
*A.* For those who were seeking the murderers of Hiram. <note>Hiram Abiff is the central character of the Masonic Third Degree legend; he was the master architect of King Solomon's Temple, murdered by three workers for refusing to reveal the secrets of a Master Mason.</note>
*Q.* Give it to me.
*A.* I cannot, Most Respectable. <note>original: "très-Reſpectable." This is the formal title used to address the presiding officer of the Lodge in these higher degrees.</note>
*Q.* Have you found the murderer?
*A.* I have punished him.
*Q.* Where did you find him?
*A.* In the dark cavern, near the fountain of Siloam. <note>Siloam (Siloé) is a pool in Jerusalem; in Masonic ritual, it serves as a symbolic location where the assassins were apprehended.</note>
*Q.* In what posture was he?
*A.* In this one, Most Respectable,
*(making the sign.)* <note>This refers to a specific ritualistic gesture or physical "sign" associated with the degree, which the candidate would perform at this moment in the ceremony.</note>

# CATECHISM
## OF THE PERFECT ELECT.

*Q.* Are you an <term>Elect</term>? <note>In the context of "High Degree" Masonry, an "Elect" (Élu) refers to a member chosen to participate in the symbolic search for and punishment of the assassins of Hiram Abiff.</note>

*A.* To rectify <term>morals</term>. <note>original: "mœurs." This refers to the Masonic goal of self-improvement and ethical conduct.</note>
*Q.* Where do the <term>Perfect Elect</term> travel?
*A.* To the <term>sovereign vault</term>. <note>The "vault" is a recurring symbol in Masonry representing a secret, sacred place where divine truth or lost knowledge is hidden.</note>

*A.* Through a long corridor, illuminated by
3, 5, 7, & 9.
*Q.* What does the number 3 signify?
*A.* The three principal <term>columns</term> S. W. B., <note>The original abbreviations "F. S. B." stand for Force, Sagesse, and Beauté (Strength, Wisdom, and Beauty). These are the three symbolic pillars that support a Masonic Lodge.</note> the age of an <term>apprentice</term>. <note>In Masonry, "age" is a symbolic number associated with one's level of initiation rather than chronological years.</note>

<term>Fellow</term>. <note>original: "Compagnon." This refers to the second degree of Masonry, the Fellowcraft.</note>

<term>Mason</term>. <note>The "nine masters" refers to a specific group of nine chosen by King Solomon in the Masonic legend to search for the master architect Hiram Abiff.</note>

<header>THIRD DEGREE</header>

*Q.* What does this posture signify?
*A.* Remorse and sadness.
*Q.* What was he called?
*A.* <term>Abiram</term>. <note>In the "Elect" degrees of Masonry, Abiram is identified as one of the three "unworthy craftsmen" who murdered the master architect Hiram Abiff. The name is often associated with the biblical figure Abiram who rebelled against Moses.</note>
*Q.* What are the tools of the <term>Perfect Elect</term>?
*A.* The hammer, the shovel, and the crowbar.
*Q.* Give me the <term>grip</term>. <note>original: "attouchement." A secret physical sign, usually a specific way of shaking hands, used to verify a member's rank.</note>
*A.* I obey, by saying N. N. M. which he pronounces. <note>These initials likely stand for "Nakam, Nakum, Meka," Hebrew for "vengeance is taken," "it shall be taken," or "vengeance for the death."</note>
*Q.* Give me the explanation of N. N. M.
*A.* Perfect.
*Q.* Give me the word of vengeance.
*A.* <term>Sterkin</term>. <note>One of the symbolic names used in high-degree Masonry for the assassins of Hiram. The names vary across different Masonic rites (e.g., Jubelo, Jubela, Jubelum or Sterkin, Oterfut, Abiram).</note>
*Q.* How old are you?
*A.* Nine years old, <term>Most Respectable</term>. <note>A Mason's "age" is symbolic and changes with each degree to represent their progress in knowledge.</note>
*Q.* Do the rays of the Sun shine upon us?
*A.* Our respectable Master is avenged.
*Q.* What is the password?
*A.* <term>Bérit-neder Aliam</term>. <note>original: "Bérit-neder Aliam." These are phonetic transliterations of Hebrew: "Berith" (Covenant), "Neder" (Vow), and "Aliam" (likely "Elohim" for God or "Aliyan" for Most High). It translates roughly to "A Covenant/Vow before God."</note>
*Q.* What is the sign?
*A.* While taking hands, turning them over twice.

## LITTLE ARCHITECT.

The Lodge shall be hung with black, to testify to the mourning felt for the loss of Hiram. <note>Hiram Abiff is the central allegorical figure of Freemasonry, a master craftsman whose death is mourned in the ritual of the "Master Mason" and subsequent degrees.</note> It is lit by seventy-two <note>original: "feptante-deux." ### ->ON FREEMASONRY<-

->E<-

The Master strikes a gavel blow as a signal for the Brothers to raise the candidate up; he unties his hands and neck, and leads him seven times around the Lodge from the <term>North: In Masonic ritual, the North represents a place of darkness or lesser light</term> to the <term>South: Representing the sun at its meridian and the height of Masonic knowledge</term>, finally arriving at the <term>West: The direction of the setting sun and the station of the Senior Warden</term>. This completed, the <term>Wardens: The principal officers of the Lodge who assist the Master in governance</term> announce him in the customary manner to the Mighty Master.

*Q.* <note>original: "D." for *Demande* (Question)</note> Did you encounter any obstacles on your journey?

*A.* <note>original: "R." for *Réponse* (Answer)</note> Yes, Mighty Master.
"It is with infinite joy," says the Master, "that I am going to reward your zeal for Masonry by conferring upon you the rank of Architect, with the direction of the works of the Temple. Approach; I shall instruct you in the mysteries of this degree."

The Warden removes the candidate's <term>apron: The primary symbol of a Mason's work and status</term>, which he throws to the ground, and leads him to the Throne from behind the Brothers of the South.

The Mighty Master, giving him the apron lined with <term>poppy-red: A deep, brilliant red color (original: *ponceau*) used in higher Masonic degrees to symbolize zeal and the fire of the spirit</term>, says to him: "The difference you notice between this apron and the one you are leaving behind announces to you that a portion of what has been told to you until now is no longer in use in our Lodges of Architects. Your occupation henceforth will be more elevated, since you will now apply yourself only to Architecture and the ornament—"

<summary>The candidate is formally elevated to the rank of Architect, undergoing a symbolic journey around the Lodge and receiving a new poppy-red apron to replace his previous one. The Master explains that this new rank shifts the Mason's focus toward the higher arts of architecture and temple ornamentation.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the instruction of the newly initiated Scottish Architect. After the presentation of the apron on the previous page, the Master completes the physical investment and proceeds to teach the secret signs and grips that allow a member to prove their rank to others.</meta>

### QUATRIEME GRADE
### ### FOURTH DEGREE

„ ...ment of the Temple. „ <note>This continues the sentence from the previous page: "Your occupation will henceforth be the management of the Temple."</note> He gives the cordon, which supports the jewel, as well as the gloves.

In this degree, we have a <term>grip: A secret handshake or manual sign of recognition (original: *attouchement*)</term>, a word, and two signs. One is called the *pass* sign; it is so named because one cannot enter our Lodges without giving it. The one who asks: "Are you a **Scottish Architect**?" immediately places his right hand on his hip on the same side, squeezing it with the thumb and index finger, while simultaneously raising his eyes to the heavens and moving his body as if he wished to draw back. The person responding does the same thing on the opposite side, saying: "I am."

The other sign, which is for general use, is performed by placing the right hand over the heart in the posture of the <term>Master's sign: A specific gesture used in the Third Degree (Master Mason) to signify fidelity</term>, then describing a diagonal line forward at the height of the face, and bringing it back to a horizontal position with the thumb resting on the forehead—which forms a <term>triangle: A geometric symbol of significant importance in the higher degrees, often representing the Delta or the Deity</term>—then letting the hand fall back into the position of the Master's sign. The response is to place the right hand flat on the right flank, making a movement as if one wished to withdraw, while passing the right foot behind the left, which forms a <term>square: A primary Masonic symbol representing morality and the proper alignment of one's actions (original: *équerre*)</term>.

<keywords>Fourth Degree, Scottish Architect, Masonic signs, pass sign, Master's sign, triangle, square, ritual gestures, recognition signals</keywords>

### ON FREEMASONRY

The <term>grip: A secret handshake or manual sign of recognition (original: *attouchement*)</term> is given like that of a Master, by quickly passing the hand under each other's elbow, which is held in the palm of the hand, and pulling each other with three tugs, pronouncing a syllable of the word **Ga-baon** each time. <note>original: "Ga-baon". This refers to Gibeon, a biblical city where the Tabernacle resided before the construction of Solomon's Temple; it is a significant "word of power" in various Masonic degrees.</note> Go now and make yourself known to all the Brothers, then you shall return to me; this is then carried out. Upon his return, the Master says to him:

> „ My Brother, after the death of **Hiram**, <note>Hiram Abiff is the legendary chief architect of Solomon's Temple. His death is the central mystery of the Master Mason degree.</note>
> „ the works were proceeding listlessly;
> „ **Solomon**, wishing to revive the ardor of the
> „ Masters, resolved to choose a new
> „ **Superintendent** of the works. For this
> „ purpose, he assembled the Masters
> „ most worthy of this position by their ge-
> „ nius, ability, and character, in the hall of
> „ his Palace. By the plans that were
> „ presented to him, he recognized that the first
> „ building had reached its perfection;
> „ he ordered them to apply the same
> „ proportions to the second elevation, and
> „ named their Master as **Architect**. These
> „ men committed themselves to it by new pro-
> „ mises, which are those you have just
> „ made, and which will associate you
> „ with their rank. May you long enjoy
> „ this happiness among us!

FOURTH DEGREE

---

# CATECHISM.

The powerful Master, making the <term>sign of passage: A specific ritual gesture used for recognition (original: *signe de passage*)</term>, says to the <term>Senior Warden: The second-highest officer in a Masonic lodge (original: *premier Surveillant*)</term>:
*Q.* Respectable Brother Senior Warden, are you an Architect?
*A.* Making the same opposite sign: powerful Master, I am.
*Q.* In what place were you received?
*A.* In the circle of the quadrature, and the <term>Holy of Holies: The innermost sanctuary of the Temple of Solomon (original: *Saint des Saints*)</term>.
*Q.* What does the circle signify?
*A.* The immensity of the <term>Grand Architect: A Masonic term for God, often abbreviated as G.A.O.T.U. (original: *grand Architecte*)</term>, who has neither beginning nor end.
*Q.* The quadrature?
*A.* The space of the <term>long square: An oblong rectangle, which is the traditional shape of a Masonic Lodge (original: *quarré long*)</term> of the tomb of **Hiram**. <note>Hiram Abiff, the legendary master builder of Solomon's Temple, whose death is central to Masonic allegory.</note>
*Q.* The Holy of Holies?
*A.* The space within the triangle where the name of the Grand Architect is engraved.
*Q.* By what means were you received as an Architect?
*A.* By the cement that was used to bind the stones of the Temple.
*Q.* What cement was used?

<summary>This page presents the ritual catechism for the Fourth Degree, explaining the symbolic significance of the circle, the "long square," and the Holy of Holies. It establishes the Architect's role through the metaphor of the cement used to build Solomon's Temple.</summary>
<keywords>Catechism, Fourth Degree, Architect, Senior Warden, Grand Architect, Holy of Holies, Hiram, quadrature, Temple of Solomon, Masonic ritual</keywords>

--- Page 112 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Catechism" or ritualized question-and-answer session for the Fourth Degree (Architect). It picks up directly from the final question on the previous page regarding the symbolic "cement" used in the Temple's construction.</meta>

->### OF MASONRY.<-

*A.* A mystic cement, composed of milk, oil, wine, and flour.

*Q.* What is the meaning of this mixture?

*A.* The milk represents sweetness, the oil represents wisdom, the wine represents strength, and the flour represents goodness; these were the respectable qualities of our Master. <note>These ingredients mirror the traditional "elements of consecration" (corn, wine, and oil) used in Masonic ceremonies, here expanded to include milk to symbolize the character of Hiram Abiff.</note>

*Q.* How was Hiram buried?

*A.* His face toward the center, his mouth upon the **letter G**, engraved within the <term>blazing star: A central Masonic symbol representing divine providence or the light of the sun (original: *étoile flamboyante*)</term>, which was set into the angles of the triangle, and connecting to the circle. <note>This description uses complex geometric symbolism—the circle, triangle, and star—to represent the spiritual perfection of the Master Builder at his rest.</note>

*Q.* In what place was he buried?

*A.* Behind the Sanctuary, in the forecourts of the Temple.

*Q.* Did Solomon leave the death of Hiram unpunished?

*A.* No: he appointed as <term>Architects: In this context, a specific rank or degree of Masonry tasked with special duties (original: *Architectes*)</term> those who wished to commit themselves, by new promises, to pursue the authors of his murder.

*Q.* How do the Architects travel?

*A.* Three times seven times, they went around **Mount Lebanon** and the nearest mountains, and visited the most secret places. <note>The number twenty-one (3x7) holds significant numerological weight in many esoteric traditions, symbolizing a complete or perfected search.</note>

*Q.* Were the assassins found?

*A.* Yes, powerful Master.

# FOURTH DEGREE

*Q.* What was the name of the one who killed Hiram?
*A.* Abiram, which signifies *assassin* or *murderer*. <note>In Masonic tradition, the assassins of Hiram Abiff are often given names starting with 'A' or 'J'; Abiram is a common variant used in the higher degrees of the 18th century.</note>
*Q.* How did you attain this degree?
*A.* By three Apprentice steps forward and backward.
*Q.* What do you occupy yourself with?
*A.* With perfect Architecture, with the care of settling disputes among the Workmen, and with working on the triangle placed upon the tomb of Hiram. <note>The "Architect" degree transitions the Mason from a physical builder to a moral administrator and judge within the lodge.</note>
*Q.* Describe the work for me.
*A.* The center contains the **letter G** and the letters **S. U. G.**
*Q.* What does the **G** signify?
*A.* *Gain* <note>original: "Gain"</note>, which signifies: *Oh You! Only Eternal.* <note>In this ritual context, "Gain" is likely a phonetic rendering of a Hebrew word for God, though its exact linguistic origin is often obscured in 18th-century manuscripts.</note>
*Q.* The **S**?
*A.* *Submission*, to let it be known that although we are <term>Architects: A specific rank in Masonry that focuses on management and design rather than manual labor (original: *Architectes*)</term>, we are subjects, and must obey our Superiors.
*Q.* The **U**?
*A.* The *Union* that must reign among us.
*Q.* The **letter G** outside the triangle?
*A.* *Gomez* <note>original: "Gomez"</note>, which means *beauty*. It is the master word, and is said to be... <note>The word "Gomez" or "Gomer" appears in several historical Masonic rites, usually associated with the concept of beauty or divine perfection.</note>

<vocab>Hiram, Abiram, Apprentice, Architecture, triangle, Gain, Submission, Union, Gomez, Masonry</vocab>

<summary>This page outlines the ritual catechism for the Fourth Degree (Architect), focusing on the symbolic meanings of the letters G, S, U, and G (Gain, Submission, Union, and Gomez). These letters represent the divine, obedience to authority, fraternal unity, and beauty within the Masonic moral framework.</summary>

Q. Are there not other letters in your Lodge?

### FIRST APARTMENT.

The Lodge requires two sets of hangings, one black and the other red, for the two stages of the reception <term>The "reception" refers to the initiation ceremony where a candidate is admitted to the degree (original: *réception*)</term>. The floor plan of the Lodge must also be similar, except that a circle must be drawn around the Holy of Holies <term>The innermost sanctuary of the Temple of Jerusalem, representing the presence of the Divine (original: *Saint des Saints*)</term>. The Master represents Solomon, and is called Most Puissant Master; the Wardens <term>Officers who assist the Master in governing the Lodge (original: *Surveillants*)</term>, Most Respectable;

the Officers, Most Venerable; and the Brothers, Most Honorable, or Grand Architects. The Master shall be placed as usual. The jewel he must wear around his neck shall be a double triangle. ### SECOND APARTMENT.

Behind the Master’s Throne, there must be a black curtain to hide what is about to be explained, which curtain is drawn and disappears when the time comes.
The Altar must be adorned with the symbols <term>Symbolic tools or emblems (original: *attributs*)</term> of Masonry, whether carved or painted; there must be seven steps, and it must be surrounded by 81 lights, arranged in a triangle. Behind the black curtain shall be a backlit transparency <term>A painting on thin fabric or paper, illuminated from behind to create a glowing effect (original: *tableau en transparent*)</term>, representing the glory of the Grand Architect surrounded by seven celestial Intelligences <note>In 18th-century esoteric thought, "Intelligences" refers to angelic beings or spirits that govern the celestial spheres.</note>; in the center shall be a luminous triangle, bearing the name of God, written in Hebrew <note>original: "nom de Dieu, écrit en Hébreu" — This typically refers to the Tetragrammaton (יהוה), the four-letter sacred name of God.</note>.

### ON MASONRY.

At the edges of the cloud, there shall be seven heads of Angels, mounted upon wings. Upon the Altar, there shall be an elevated representation of the Ark of the Covenant, supported by two Cherubim who cover it with their wings. The front of the Ark must be sculpted with the Lamb of Life resting upon a Book, from which the seven seals emerge. To the right side of the Ark, on the front, shall be the Brazen Sea <term>A large ritual basin for washing, modeled after the one in Solomon's Temple (original: *Mer d’airain*)</term>, supported by twelve golden oxen. If it is desired that the Lodge be arranged with the utmost precision, one must place the ten Urns, five on each side, and the Sea at the end, toward the West, tending toward the South. To the South shall be placed a bronze table, called the Altar of Burnt Offerings <term>The place where sacrificial offerings were ritually consumed (original: *Autel des holocaustes*)</term>. The seven-branched candlestick <note>This refers to the Menorah described in the Book of Exodus.</note> shall be opposite the Ark. Beside the North shall be a Table, called the Table of Showbread <term>The twelve loaves of bread traditionally placed on a sacred table in the Tabernacle or Temple (original: *Pains de proposition*)</term>, upon which eleven loaves shall be placed one upon another, whether real or symbolic. Vases, cups, and urns for offerings shall also be placed there.

### P R E P A R A T I O N .

The Master of Ceremonies must remove all offensive and defensive weapons from the Candidate <term>The initiate seeking the degree (original: *Récipiendaire*)</term>, without demanding his jewelry <note>In the lower degrees, candidates are often stripped of all "metals" (money and jewelry) to symbolize poverty and humility. Here, only weapons—symbols of worldly strife—are removed.</note>, and have him sign his name on the tracing board <term>A symbolic illustration or register used during the ceremony (original: *planche à tracer*)</term>, at the very bottom of the list of Brothers. He blindfolds him and delivers a discourse on the degree he is about to receive, declaring to him that he is destined to replace Hiram <note>Hiram Abiff, the central figure of Masonic legend and the master builder of Solomon's Temple.</note>, whose loss is still mourned; but that he must be presented to Solomon and to the Grand Architect; that this is the moment when he must arm himself with firmness, since, for lack of courage, he would be excluded forever; that nevertheless, this degree contains nothing that should frighten him more than the preceding ones, and thus he should have full confidence in himself.

---

### OPENING OF THE LODGE.

The Master strikes one blow, and says:
"My Brothers, help me to open the [Lodge]." <note>The text cuts off here as the opening dialogue begins.</note>

# FIFTH GRADE

*The Most Puissant.* My Brother, what are the fruits of your journey?

*The Candidate.* <term>Recipient: The person being initiated; the candidate.</term> Most Puissant, I have surveyed the plan of the Temple’s works, and I bring the designs to you.

*The Most Puissant.* My Brother, present them to me by the three steps of a Fellowcraft <note>In Masonic ritual, a "Fellowcraft" (Compagnon) is the second degree; the "steps" refer to a specific ritualized way of walking toward the East.</note>.

*The Candidate* presents them in such a way that the third step brings him to the foot of the Throne, where he places his right knee on the ground.

*The Most Puissant* takes the tracing board <note>Original: "Planche à tracer." A board used by the Master to draw the plans for the workmen; in this context, it represents the candidate's proficiency.</note> from him, examines it, and passes it to his right to the Brother nearest him, who has it passed around the Lodge from hand to hand until it returns to the Most Puissant Master. Having received it, he places the Candidate’s hand upon the square and the double triangle <note>The double triangle, or hexagram, is a significant symbol in the higher degrees, often representing the union of the spiritual and material worlds.</note> which are upon the Altar, the compass upon the wrist, and the sword over them; he then has him take his obligation in these terms:

> ,, tions that I have taken in my pre-
> ,, vious Masonic grades, never to
> ,, reveal the secrets of the Grand
> ,, Architects, to any Architect, Master,

### *I N S T R U C T I O N .*

Q. <note>original: *D.* for *Demande* (Question).</note> Brother, Most Respectable Senior Warden, are you a Grand Architect?
A. <note>original: *R.* for *Réponse* (Answer).</note> Mighty Master, I have seen the great light of the third Apartment. <note>Masonic rituals often divided the symbolic Temple into different "apartments" or sections; the third represents a high level of initiation.</note>
Q. Where were you received as a Grand Architect?
A. In the High Place, and the Middle Chamber.
Q. Why do you name it so?
A. Because it was the place where Solomon worked on the plan of the Temple with the Superintendent of the works. <note>In Masonic lore, the Superintendent of the works often refers to Hiram Abiff, the master builder.</note>
Q. What do you occupy yourself with in the Degree of Grand Architect?
A. With building the final edifice, or the...

# FIFTH DEGREE

...third section <note>The French *corps* here refers to the architectural division or part of the structure mentioned on the previous page.</note> which forms the summit of the building, to erecting Tabernacles, and to furnishing them with precious and consecrated ornaments.

*Q.* <note>original: *D.* for *Demande* (Question).</note> What are the ornaments?
*A.* <note>original: *R.* for *Réponse* (Answer).</note> The Ark of the Covenant, supported by two Cherubim who cover it with their wings; the bronze Table, that of the Burnt Offerings, that of the Bread <note>A reference to the Table of Shewbread or "Bread of the Presence" described in Exodus.</note>, and the seven-branched Candlestick.

*Q.* What does the Ark of the Covenant contain?
*A.* The *Stekenna* <note>original: *Stekenna*, a phonetic transcription of the Hebrew *Shekhinah* (שכינה), referring to the dwelling or settling of the divine presence of God.</note>, who settled himself between the Cherubim who cover him with their wings in the Holy of Holies, on the day of dedication, where he delivered his oracles.

*Q.* What was the principal Law given by the *Stekenna*?
*A.* That which was given on Mount Sinai, kept since then in the Ark, which is the first written Law.

*Q.* Give me the sign of this Law.
*A.* It is given by placing both hands on the head, fingers open, which represents the symbol of the ten Commandments. <note>The ten fingers spread over the head are meant to visually represent the two stone tablets of the Decalogue.</note>

*Q.* In what form is the *Stekenna* represented?
*A.* In the form of a lamb tran- <note>The word is cut off at the end of the page; it likely continues as "slaughtered" (*tranché* or *égorgé*) or "pierced" (*transpercé*), common symbols for the Paschal lamb in high-degree Masonry.</note>

quil, lying down, resting on the Book of the Seven Seals. <note>The word completes "tranquil" from the previous page's "tran-". This refers to the "Lamb of God" from the Book of Revelation, often depicted resting on a book with seven seals.</note>

*Q.* <note>original: *D.* for *Demande* (Question).</note> Of what wood was the Ark constructed?
*A.* <note>original: *R.* for *Réponse* (Answer).</note> Of Shittim wood <note>original: *Séthim*; the Hebrew *shittah* refers to acacia wood, prized in the Bible for its durability.</note>, an incorruptible wood, overlaid with plates of gold.
*Q.* What were its proportions?
*A.* It was two and a half cubits in length, half a cubit in width, and the same in height. <note>The text specifies "one half" (*une demie*), though the biblical description in Exodus 25:10 typically lists the width and height as one and a half cubits.</note>
*Q.* What was its shape?
*A.* That of a square chest, with four gold rings; and four staves of Shittim wood to carry it, and it was covered with plates of gold.
*Q.* What did it contain?
*A.* The *Stekenna* <note>original: *Stekenna*, referring to the *Shekhinah* or divine glory.</note>, the Tables of the Law, Aaron’s Rod, and the Urn full of manna.
*Q.* In what place was it made, and by whom?
*A.* It was made at Horeb <note>original: *Oreb*.</note> by Bezalel and Oholiab <note>original: *Bescel* and *Oliob*; the biblical craftsmen chosen by God to build the Tabernacle.</note>. It left Horeb and passed through Moab, from Moab to Shechem <note>original: *Sichem*.</note>, then to Shiloh <note>original: *Silo*.</note>, from Shiloh to the Temple of Dagon <note>A reference to the Philistine capture of the Ark.</note>, from there into the house of Abinadab <note>original: *Abmadab*.</note>, from there to that of Obed-Edom <note>original: *Obededon*.</note>, then to Kiriath-Jearim <note>original: *Cariathiarim*.</note>, from Kiriath-Jearim to Jerusalem, and finally to holy Zion in the Temple.

### FIFTH DEGREE

*Q.* <note>original: *D.* for *Demande* (Question).</note> Where were the other ornaments placed, and what purpose did they serve?
*A.* <note>original: *R.* for *Réponse* (Answer).</note> The Bronze Table or the Grated Altar was in front, near the Mercy Seat <note>original: *Propitiatoire*; the gold lid of the Ark of the Covenant, considered the throne of God's presence.</note>, from where God spoke; the Altar of Incense and the Altar of Burnt Offerings <note>original: *Holocaustes*; refers to religious animal sacrifices that were completely consumed by fire.</note>, each on one side, served for offerings and victims. The Table of Showbread <note>original: *Pains de proposition*; twelve loaves of bread placed weekly in the Tabernacle as an offering.</note> was in front, as was the Seven-branched Candlestick, which was of pure gold. Most [of the furnishings] were made of Shittim wood <note>original: *Séthim*; acacia wood.</note>, covered with plates of gold and bronze, adorned with the most precious jewels and of exquisite workmanship.
*Q.* How did you come to know all these things?
*A.* By the plan of the Temple’s works, which I surveyed on the tracing board <note>The tracing board is a symbolic blueprint used by Masons to design their spiritual and moral "building."</note> during a very arduous journey.
*Q.* What was the duration of this journey?
*A.* Three times twenty-seven circuits around the works.
*Q.* What does this arduous journey signify?
*A.* The construction of the work.
*Q.* What is your age? <note>In Masonry, "age" refers to the symbolic progress or seniority within the degrees of the order.</note>
*A.* As an Apprentice 3 years, as a Fellow Craft <note>original: *Compagnon*.</note> 6, as a Master 9, as a Little Architect 27, and as a Grand Architect 31 years.
*Q.* Was light the reward for your labor?

<meta>catchword: A. No :</meta>

# OF MASONRY.

*A.* <note>original: *R.* for *Réponse* (Answer).</note> No: Most Puissant <note>A traditional title of respect for the presiding officer of a Masonic lodge in the higher degrees.</note>, three much stronger trials earned it for me.
*Q.* <note>original: *D.* for *Demande* (Question).</note> What are these trials?
*A.* The first was three Fellow Craft <note>original: *Compagnon*.</note> steps backward, to make us realize that we must not lack firmness in the path of virtue, despite the humiliations that may be required of us in this life.
The second, a surprising freedom, or a release from all the bonds that might have attached me to the Order, so as to bring me to the third without fear of my indiscretion.
The third was a voluntary engagement that my heart admitted, my soul acknowledged, & that my mouth pronounced.
*Q.* What did this engagement produce for you?
*A.* An homage to the shades <note>original: *mânes*; in Roman mythology, the *manes* were the spirits of the dead. In Masonry, this refers to honoring the memory of the legendary architect Hiram Abiff.</note> of Hiram to weep for his death.
*Q.* What did this grief produce for you?
*A.* A word marked by a second circumference, an image of infinite power, which predicts sadness and sorrow in Masonry; the collapse of our material edifice, and the spirit of a more perfect work through obedience.
*Q.* What is this word?

G <note>A printer's signature mark used to guide the binder in assembling the pages of the book.</note>
