98 FIFTH DEGREE

*A.* <note>original: *R.* for *Réponse* (Answer).</note> *Civi*, which means, *bend the knee.*

*Q.* <note>original: *D.* for *Demande* (Question).</note> Was this sorrow long?

*A.* After seven minutes, *ki* was pronounced, that is to say, *arise.*

*Q.* What resulted from this word?

*A.* Surprise, brilliance & the shining light, and the proclamation.

*Q.* What was this surprise?

*A.* My sight passed from this surprise to the purple & the hyacinth. <note>These colors refer to the traditional veils of the Temple of Solomon; "hyacinth" (original: *hyacinte*) in this context usually refers to a specific shade of blue or violet-blue cloth mentioned in biblical descriptions of the Tabernacle.</note>

*Q.* What is this brilliance, this shining light?

*A.* The <term>Stekenna</term> <note>original: *Stekenna*; this is a phonetic transcription of the Hebrew *Shekinah*, which represents the divine presence or the "dwelling" of the glory of God.</note>, the glory of the Grand Architect.

*Q.* To what purpose did this proclamation serve?

*A.* The recognition that all the Brothers made of my person to replace Hiram, & declaring me Grand Architect & Superintendent of the Temple works, by naming me <term>Moabon</term> <note>Moabon is a ritual name used in high-degree Masonry, often interpreted in this context as "Son of the Father" or "the architect's successor."</note>.

*Q.* In what place did you hold your first Lodge?

*A.* Between the three Mountains inaccessible to the <term>Profane</term> <note>original: *Profanes*; a term used by Masons to describe those who have not been initiated into their secrets.</note>, where no cock has ever crowed, no lion roared, nor woman cackled, in a deep valley.

*Q.* How do you name these three Mountains?

<summary>This page describes the ritual elevation of the candidate to the rank of Grand Architect, succeeding the fallen master Hiram, and introduces the concept of the Stekenna (Shekinah) or divine light. It also establishes the symbolic, secluded location where the first Lodge of this degree was allegedly held.</summary>

### OF MASONRY.

*A.* <note>original: *R.* for *Réponse* (Answer).</note> Mount Moriah, within the grounds of Gibeon <note>original: *Gabaon*; Gibeon is the biblical location where King Solomon offered sacrifices before the Temple was built.</note>; Mount Sinai & the mountain of Hérédon.

*Q.* <note>original: *D.* for *Demande* (Question).</note> What is this mountain of Hérédon?

*A.* A mountain located between the West & the North of Scotland, at the end of the Sun’s course <note>i.e., the North-West, symbolizing the furthest reaches of the known world or a place of symbolic sunset.</note>, where the first Lodge of Masonry was held; in that part of the earth which gave the name to **Scottish Masonry**.

*Q.* What do you mean by **deep valley**?

*A.* I mean the tranquility of our Lodges.

*Q.* What can produce this tranquility?

*A.* The preservation of our marks since their origin.

*Q.* What are the marks of the **Grand Architect**?

*A.* A sign, a grip <term>attouchement</term> <note>original: *attouchement*; in Masonry, this refers to a specific way of touching hands or arms to recognize a fellow member.</note>, a word.

*Q.* Give me the sign.

*A.* *He gives it.*

*Q.* What do you call this grip?

*A.* The perfect proof.

*Q.* Give me the word.

### FIFTH DEGREE, etc.

*A.* <note>original: *R.* for *Réponse* (Answer). In Masonic ritual, sacred words are often spelled out letter by letter or syllable by syllable rather than spoken aloud to maintain secrecy.</note> I will spell it with you.

*Q.* <note>original: *D.* for *Demande* (Question).</note> What does this word signify?

*A.* The name of the Grand Architect.

**The Most Puissant to the Wardens.**
"Very respectable Brothers, Senior and Junior Wardens, give notice, each on your side, that I am going to close the Lodge of Grand Architect." They do so.

*Q.* What time is it, Brother Senior Warden?

*A.* Puissant Master <note>The title "Puissant" (meaning powerful or mighty) is a traditional honorific for the Master presiding over this degree.</note>, the last instant, the last hour, the last day that the Grand Architect of the Universe was occupied with the creation of the world. Here is the last instant, the last hour, the last day that Solomon employed in constructing the Temple.

**The Most Puissant Master.** "Here is the last day that I have held Lodge; it is time to close it and to rest."

The Wardens repeat, each on their side. The Master strikes seven blows <note>The number seven is highly symbolic in this degree, representing completion and the seven years taken to build Solomon’s Temple.</note> in the manner of a Grand Architect, and says: "My Brothers, the Lodge is closed." The Wardens repeat, and all the Brothers make the ordinary acclamations <note>Usually involving "Huzzah" or "Vivat," accompanied by rhythmic clapping.</note>.

->End of the Fifth Degree.<-

# SIXTH DEGREE

surrounded by enemies on all sides, they worked to reconstruct the Temple and the walls of the City of Jerusalem, holding a sword in one hand and a trowel in the other; this gave rise to the naming of this Degree *Knight of the Sword*, or *Free Mason Knight*, as men chosen to ensure the safety of the subordinate laborers; yet, to maintain equality with their Brothers, they were no less occupied with the common works. <note>This "sword and trowel" imagery is a central motif in the Knight of the East degrees, symbolizing the need to defend the faith while building the community.</note>

It is also called *Knight of the East*, because the institution was established in that part of the world. They are called Free Masons: 1st. Because it is conferred with an open face. <note>In many early Masonic degrees, the candidate was blindfolded; "open face" suggests the candidate has already proven their worth and sees the light of the degree clearly.</note> 2nd. Because among the captives, there was a class of Masons descended from the lineage of King Hiram, of Moabon <note>Moabon is a term found in high-degree Masonry, sometimes used as a sacred word or referring to a specific ancestral line linked to the building of the first Temple.</note>, and of the first Architects, whom Solomon had exempted from all taxes, and who were the first chosen by Zerubbabel <note>original: "Jérobabel"</note>.

The candidate is named Zerubbabel, because they represent the Prince who receives orders from Cyrus <note>Cyrus the Great, the Persian King who liberated the Jews from Babylon.</note> for the rebuilding of the Temple.

The Lodge must have two Apartments, and in each, a tableau <note>A "tableau" refers to a tracing board or symbolic painting illustrating the degree's themes.</note> and a decoration.

# ON MASONRY.

## FIRST APARTMENT.

It must represent the Apartment where the Court of Cyrus is held, King of the Assyrians <note>Historically, Cyrus the Great was the King of the Persians; Masonic rituals of this era occasionally used titles symbolically or followed older historical traditions.</note>, reigning in Babylon. It must be hung in green and illuminated by 70 lights, to mark the 70 years of captivity <note>The 70 years of Babylonian captivity are central to the biblical narrative of the Jewish exile.</note>. There must be a Throne in the East for the Master, seats in the South for the Brothers, and another in the West for the Warden. The square of the Lodge must be enclosed by a small wall made of cardboard or wood, a foot and a half in height, painted in white, green, and red squares, to represent the walls of Babylon. When the Brothers are standing, they must be inside the square, and outside when they are seated. This wall shall be furnished with seven towers: three in the South, three in the North, and one in the West. The six towers shall be a foot and a half above the walls; but the one in the center of the West must be seven feet high, and its circumference shall be proportioned to

The wall hanging of the West must join exactly at the sides of this exterior part, so that one can enter and exit without seeing anything. This door shall be guarded by two Brothers with swords at their sides and pikes in their hands. The Chamber shall be adorned with a Throne, which shall be placed on the line of the wall of the East, so that one is thus within the interior of the square; behind the Throne there shall be a transparency <note>A "transparent" was a painting on thin fabric or paper, illuminated from behind to create a glowing effect, popular in 18th-century ritual theater.</note> representing the dream of Cyrus: namely, a furious and roaring lion, ready to pounce upon him; higher up, the Glory of the Grand Architect <note>A standard Masonic reference to the Divine, often depicted as a radiant triangle or eye within a sunburst.</note> upon a luminous cloud. Below shall be Nebuchadnezzar and Belshazzar <note>The predecessors of Cyrus; their depiction in chains symbolizes the fall of the Babylonian empire and the end of the Jewish exile.</note>, predecessors of Cyrus, laden with chains. From the center of the Glory shall emerge an eagle, which will carry in its beak these words: *Set the captives free*. Behind the tower of the West, there must be water where, by agitating it, one can represent the river Starburzanay <note>original: "Starburzanay." This is a ritual corruption of the name Shethar-boznai, a Persian official mentioned in the Book of Ezra who questioned the rebuilding of the Temple.</note>; over the river shall be a solid wooden bridge, which will lead to the second Apartment.

# SECOND APARTMENT.

It represents the enclosure or the ground on which the Second Temple <note>The Temple of Jerusalem, rebuilt by Zerubbabel after the Babylonian exile.</note> was built. The Temple must appear there in all its splendor, and the wall hangings shall be red. The decoration will be the same as that of the Grand Architect <note>This likely refers to the "Glory of the Grand Architect" mentioned on the previous page: a radiant sunburst or triangle representing the Divine.</note>.

**Title of the First Apartment.** The Master represents Cyrus <note>Cyrus the Great, the Persian King who decreed the liberation of the Jews.</note>, and is called **Sovereign Master**. The Senior Warden represents Nebuzaradan <note>original: "Nabuzardin." Historically the captain of Nebuchadnezzar's guard, here repurposed as a general in Cyrus's court.</note>, his first General; the Junior Warden represents General Mithridates <note>original: "Mitridate." Likely referring to Mithredath, the Persian treasurer mentioned in the Book of Ezra.</note>; the Secretary is called the **Chancellor**; the Master of Ceremonies is called **Grand Master**; and the Knight Brothers are called **Masons**.

**Title of the Second Apartment.** The Master is called **Most Excellent Master**, or **Excellence of the Order**; the Wardens are called **Most Powerful**; the Brothers are called **Most Venerable**; and the Candidate <note>original: "le Récipiendaire."</note> is called **Zerubbabel** <note>original: "Jérobabel." The prince of Judah who led the first group of captives back to Jerusalem to rebuild the Temple.</note>.

# CLOTHING OF THE BROTHERS

### *in the first Apartment.*

THE Master and the Officers wear around the neck a wide moiré <note>A fabric, usually silk, with a wavy, "watered" appearance.</note> green ribbon, falling to a point over the stomach without a jewel. The Master holds a sceptre, and the Brothers hold their swords in their hands. The Wardens and the Brothers will have a wide moiré green ribbon, worn across the shoulder from left to right without a jewel, plus an apron lined with green taffeta, bordered with a small ribbon of the same color, without any other symbol, and with the flap <note>original: "bavette." The triangular top portion of a Masonic apron.</note> down; the apron and the ribbon may only be worn during the ceremonies that take place in the first Apartment, because these are secular <note>original: "profanes." In a Masonic context, this refers to the non-Masonic world or honors granted by those outside the Order.</note> marks that Cyrus would have wanted to give to the Members of Solomon, believing that it was sufficient to make them Masons. They are worn on this day, however, in memory of the permission that this Prince and his Court granted to Zerubbabel <note>original: "Jérobabel." The leader of the Jewish people who led the first group of captives back from Babylon to Jerusalem.</note> to rebuild the Temple. # ON FREEMASONRY.

The Generals repeat. The Sovereign strikes seven blows, with a pause between the 5th and 6th <note>This indicates a rhythmic "battery" of knocks, specifically five followed by a pause, then two more (|||||  ||).</note>, and the Generals do the same. The Sovereign General says:

*Senior Warden.* <note>original: "Premier Surveillant." While the Sovereign addresses the "Senior Warden" (a standard Masonic officer), the response on this page comes from the "First General," reflecting the military-court setting of this specific degree.</note> What is the first care of a Mason?

*The First General.* Sovereign Master, it is to see if the Lodge is well tiled <note>original: "bien couverte." In Masonry, to "tile" a lodge means to ensure the doors are closed and guarded so that no uninitiated people can hear the proceedings.</note>. He sees to it. After having examined the exterior and interior, and having assured himself of the Brothers, the second General says: “ Sovereign
,, Master, the Lodge is well tiled,
,, and all the Brothers here present are Che-
,, valiers of the Sword <term>Knights of the Sword: A degree also known as the Knight of the East, which commemorates the Israelites' return from Babylon to Jerusalem to rebuild the Temple.</term>.

*Q. The Sovereign to the first General.* At what time are we?

*A. The First General.* The day of the 70 years of captivity is accomplished <note>Refers to the Biblical prophecy (Jeremiah 25:11) that the Jewish people would remain in exile in Babylon for seventy years.</note>.

**Q.** Concerning what?
**A.** To ask for mercy for my Brothers, who have been in servitude for 70 years <note>This refers to the 70 years of the Babylonian Captivity, a central historical and symbolic timeframe in this degree.</note>.

**A.** That, under the favor of the Grand Architect of the Universe <term>Grand Architect of the Universe: A universal term for the Supreme Being used in Freemasonry to allow men of different faiths to join together in prayer and ritual.</term>, the King's justice might grant us liberty, and that he might permit us to go and rebuild the Temple of our God.

*The Sovereign.* Since such just motives lead him here, let the liberty to appear before us, with his face uncovered <note>Candidates in these rituals are often blindfolded or "hoodwinked" to represent their state of darkness or captivity; uncovering the face symbolizes a transition toward freedom and light.</note>, be granted to him.

*R.* <note>The "R" stands for "Response," here spoken by the candidate, Zerubbabel.</note> Sovereign Master, when Solomon gave us its first principles, he taught us that equality must be the primary motive. # SIXTH DEGREE of the Scottish sash, to which the Jewel is attached.

### WHILE GIVING HIM THE JEWEL.

This Jewel, by the addition of the crossed swords <note>original: "épées en sautoir"; a heraldic term referring to two swords placed in the shape of an 'X' or saltire</note>, announces to us the trophy of our Masonry: you must use your own only for her, that is to say, for the sake of equity.

### WHILE GIVING HIM THE GLOVES.

We shall now proceed to your proclamation: "My Brother Knight Masons, do you consent that Zerubbabel <note>Zerubbabel was the governor of the Persian Province of Judah and the grandson of King Jehoiachin; he led the first group of Jews back from the Babylonian captivity and laid the foundation of the Second Temple in Jerusalem</note> shall henceforth reign over the works of Masonry?" They all give their assent by lowering and raising the points of their swords; he is then placed in the chair destined for him, while being told: "Pass, my Brother, to the Tribunal <term>Tribunal: In this context, the governing body or the seating area for the highest-ranking officers of the Lodge</term> of the Sovereigns of our Lodges. You shall serve as the triangular stone <note>Similar to a "cornerstone" or "keystone," the triangular stone represents the essential spiritual and structural foundation of the Masonic Temple</note> to the Building; you shall reign over the Workers, just as Solomon, Hiram, and Moabon <note>Moabon is a symbolic name used in various high-degree Masonic rites, often associated with the discovery of the Master's grave or the preservation of ancient secrets</note> reigned there, by commanding over them." As soon as he is placed, the Brothers sheathe their swords and clap their hands three times...

## I N S T R U C T I O N.

*Q.* Brother Senior Warden, how were you promoted to the eminent Grade of Knight of the Sword?
*A.* I attained it through humility, patience, and frequent solicitations.
*Q.* To whom did you address yourself?
*A.* To the Great King <note>This refers to Cyrus the Great, the Persian King who, according to the biblical account and Masonic legend, released the Jews from their seventy years of captivity in Babylon</note>.
*Q.* What is your name?
*A.* *Zerubbabel*.
*Q.* Your Country?
*A.* Judea: I was born of noble parents, of the Tribe of Judah.
*Q.* What art do you profess?
*A.* Masonry.
*Q.* What building are you constructing?
*A.* Temples and Tabernacles <term>Tabernacle: Historically, a portable dwelling place for the divine presence; in Masonry, it represents a sacred space for spiritual work</term>.
*Q.* Where are you building them?
*A.* For lack of ground, we build them in our hearts <note>This marks a transition from operative masonry (building physical structures) to speculative masonry (building internal character)</note>.
*Q.* What is the name of a Knight Mason?
*A.* That of a most-free Mason <note>original: "Maçon très-libre"</note>.
*Q.* Why most-free?
*A.* Because the Masons who were...

### SIXTH DEGREE

chosen by Solomon to work on the Temple, were declared free and exempt from all taxes for themselves and their descendants. They also had the privilege of carrying arms. Upon the destruction of the Temple by Nebuchadnezzar <note>Nebuchadnezzar II, the Babylonian king who destroyed Solomon's Temple in 586 BCE</note>, they were taken into captivity with the Jewish People; but the kindness of King Cyrus gave them permission to rebuild a second Temple under Zerubbabel <note>original: "Jérobabel"; the leader of the Jewish people who led the first wave of exiles back from Babylon</note>, and set them at liberty. It is from this era that we bear the name of Free Masons.
*Q.* Was the ancient Temple beautiful?
*A.* It was the first wonder of the world in richness and grandeur; for its courtyard could hold two hundred thousand people.
*Q.* Who was the principal Architect who constructed this great Edifice?
*A.* God was the first, Solomon the second, and Hiram <note>Hiram Abiff, the legendary master craftsman of the Temple</note> the third.
*Q.* Who laid the first stone?
*A.* Solomon.
*Q.* At what hour was it laid?
*A.* Before sunrise.
*Q.* Why?
*A.* To make known the vigilance that we must have for the service of the <term>Architect of the Universe: A non-denominational Masonic term for the Divine, often abbreviated as G.A.O.T.U.</term>.

### ON MASONRY.
->I<-

Q. What cement was used there?
A. A mystical cement, composed of flour, milk, oil, and wine. <note>These ingredients are traditionally used in Masonic "consecration" ceremonies, symbolizing nourishment, refreshment, and joy.</note>

Q. Explain the mystical meaning to me?
A. To form the first man, the <term>Supreme Being: A non-sectarian term for God used frequently in 18th-century philosophy and Freemasonry</term> employed gentleness, wisdom, strength, and goodness.

Q. Where was the first stone laid?
A. In the middle of the Chamber intended for the Sanctuary.

Q. How many parts did the ancient Temple have?
A. Three: one to the West, one to the South, and one to the North.

Q. How long did the Temple last?
A. 470 years, 6 months, and 10 days.

Q. Under which King of Israel was it destroyed?
A. Under the reign of <note>Zedekiah (original: "Sédécias"), the last king of Judah before the destruction of Jerusalem by the Babylonians</note>, the last of the line of David.

Q. What is the meaning of the broken Column <term>Boaz: One of the two great pillars at the porch of Solomon's Temple; its name is often translated as "In strength"</term>, and the seven-branched <term>Candlestick: Referring to the Menorah, the sacred lampstand of the Temple</term> being taken away?
A. It represents the confusion and evil committed when one admits someone who is unworthy; it is like removing a limb from the Order.

Q. Why is the number 81 held in such great veneration among Masons?

# SIXTH DEGREE

A. Because this number explains the triple essence of the Divinity, represented by the triple triangle, by the **square of nine**, and the number **three**. <note>The number 81 (9x9) is significant in high-degree Masonry, often symbolizing the completion of a cycle or a divine return.</note>

Q. Why are the chains of the Captives triangular?

A. The Assyrians, having learned that the triangle was among them the emblem of the name of the Eternal, had the chains fashioned in this way to cause even more grief to the Captives. <note>This refers to the psychological cruelty of forcing prisoners to wear their sacred symbol—representing God—as the very instrument of their enslavement.</note>

Q. Why was it forbidden for Masons to work on <term>profane: In a Masonic context, this refers to anything outside the order or secular in nature</term> buildings?

A. To teach us not to frequent <term>irregular Lodges: Groups claiming to be Masons that operate without a legitimate charter or "warrant" from a recognized Grand Lodge</term>.

Q. What was the plan that Cyrus gave for the new Temple?

A. 120 <term>cubits: An ancient unit of length based on the forearm, roughly 18 inches</term> in depth, 60 in height, and the same in width.

Q. Why did Cyrus order that wood be cut from the forests of Lebanon, and that stones be drawn from the quarries of Tyre, for the construction of the new Temple?

A. Because it was necessary that the second Temple be in every way similar to the first. <note>Tyre and Lebanon were the sources of materials for Solomon's original Temple; using the same sources for the Second Temple signifies continuity and the restoration of the sacred covenant.</note>

Q. Give me the name of the principal

->### ON MASONRY.<-

Architect who had the direction of this second Temple?
*A.* Bibot is his name. <note>While Zerubbabel is the historical figure usually associated with the rebuilding of the Temple in the Bible, various Masonic rites use specific names like "Bibot" or "Bebai" drawn from genealogical lists in the books of Ezra or Nehemiah.</note>
*Q.* Why the sword that the Workers carry while working?
*A.* It is because while they worked with one hand to carry materials and rebuild the Temple, as they were subject to the incursions of their Enemies, they held their swords ready to defend their work and their Brothers. <note>This is a direct reference to Nehemiah 4:17: "Those who built on the wall, and those who carried burdens, loaded themselves so that with one hand they worked at construction, and with the other held a weapon."</note>
*Q.* Why the 70 lights in the Lodge?
*A.* In memory of the 70 years of the Babylonian captivity.
*Q.* Are you a Knight of the Sword?
*A. *Look at me; he puts his hand to his sword.*
*Q.* Give me the sign.
*A. *He does so.*
*Q.* Give me the word and the password.
*A.* Judas, Benjamin, Libertas. <note>original: "Libertas" is Latin for "Liberty" or "Freedom."</note>
*Q.* Give the <term>grip: A specific method of touching or shaking hands used as a secret sign of recognition between members</term> to the Brother Senior Warden.
*A. *He gives it to him.*
*Q.* Where have you worked?
*A.* At the rebuilding of the second Temple.

## PRUSSIAN KNIGHT.

*The very ancient Order of the Noachites <note>The Noachites (descendants of Noah) represent a branch of Masonry that focuses on the period between the Great Flood and the building of the Tower of Babel, emphasizing themes of humility and divine justice.</note>, known under the name of Prussian Knights, translated from the German by Mr. de Berage <note>Berage was a pseudonym for a Masonic author active in the mid-18th century; he is often associated with the early dissemination of these "High Degrees" in France.</note>, Knight of Eloquence of the Lodge of Mr. de Saint-Gellair, Knight Commander-Lieutenant, Inspector General of Prussian Lodges in France, in the year of the Order 4658 <note>Masonic calendars often use "Anno Lucis" (Year of Light) or other specific epochs. This date likely corresponds to 1758 AD in the Gregorian calendar.</note>.*

The Grand Master General of the Order, who is named Grand Knight

--- Page 183 ---
<meta>This page continues the introduction of the Seventh Degree (Noachite or Prussian Knight), moving from the title page and translation credits to the legendary history and governance of the Order. It establishes the King of Prussia as the order's protector and details the ritual importance of the full moon and the Tower of Babel.</meta>

# SEVENTH DEGREE

Commander is the most illustrious Brother Frederick of Brunswick, King of Prussia <note>While Frederick the Great was a famous Freemason, he was of the House of Hohenzollern; the text may be conflating him with his contemporary, Duke Ferdinand of Brunswick, who was a prominent leader in European Masonry.</note>. His ancestors, for 300 years, have been protectors of this Order, whose Knights celebrate the memory of the destruction of the Tower of Babel. Formerly they were known under the name of *Noachites*, that is to say, descendants of Noah. The Pagans know them by the name of *Titans*, who wanted to scale Heaven to dethrone *Jupiter*. But the Prussians, who know no other God than the Grand Architect of the Universe <note>A universal, non-denominational term for a supreme being, used in Masonry to encompass all religious faiths.</note>, find their happiness in glorifying Him, and in celebrating every year, during the night of the full moon of March, the confusion of languages and the disunion of the workers of the Tower of Babel, which is one of the great wonders of the Creator, because it is the era of that day of vengeance. This is also why they assemble in a secluded place on the night of the full moon of each month to hold Lodge, and they can only receive proselytes <note>New initiates or converts to the degree.</note> by the light of the moon.

The Grand Master General of the Order is called Knight Commander-Lieutenant: the Officers are, the first Knight of Office; the second, Knight

# OF MASONRY.

->I iv<-

of Office, Introducer; the third, Knight of Eloquence <note>The "Knight of Eloquence" typically serves as the Orator of the Lodge, responsible for delivering lectures and ensuring the laws are followed.</note>; the fourth, Knight of the Guard; the fifth, Knight of the Chancery; the sixth, Knight of Finance. The other Masters of the Lodge are called Prussian Knight Masons. The Lodge must be composed of at least the first three Knights; but this is only in the case that there is a shortage of <note>original: "M. P."</note> Prussian Knight Masons in the City where the Lodge is held; one could then do without the others.

The <term>Noachites</term> <note>Followers or descendants of Noah.</note>, named today Prussian Knights, descend from <term>Phaleg</term> <note>In the Bible, Peleg (whose name means "division"); in Masonic legend, he is often identified as the architect of the Tower of Babel.</note>, Grand Architect of the Tower of Babel; thus their Order draws its origin from further back than the Masons descending from Hiram: for the Tower of Babel was built several centuries before the Temple of Solomon, and in former times, it was not required that the candidates <note>original: "Sujets"</note> who proposed themselves to be received be Masons descending from Hiram <note>Hiram Abiff, the central figure of the first three degrees of Freemasonry, who served as the architect of Solomon's Temple.</note>. But from the time of the Crusades, when all the Knights of the different Orders of Europe were initiated by the Christian & Confederate Princes to conquer Palestine, the Masons descending from Hiram (whom we shall hereafter call <term>Hiramites</term>) by ref—

# SEVENTH DEGREE

[res]pect for the Order of the <term>Noachites</term> <note>Followers or descendants of Noah, whose order predates the Solomonic Temple in this tradition.</note>, which was held in great veneration at that time, were received into it. The Prussian Knights, out of gratitude, believing they could not better entrust their mysteries than to the descendants of Hiram, have since required that all candidates <note>original: "Récipiendaires"</note> be received Masters of that Order, without being able to admit any others. This is established in the Statutes of the Order, which are held in the Archives of the **King of Prussia** <note>Frederick the Great, the legendary patron of these degrees.</note>, by which it is expressly forbidden for a Prussian Knight Mason to receive any Candidate unless he has first given proof of his zeal and his capacity in the Order of the Masters descending from Hiram <note>Commonly called "Hiramites," referring to the standard three degrees of Craft Masonry based on the legend of Hiram Abiff.</note>. He must prove that he has performed the duties of a **Dignitary Officer** in a complete and regular Lodge.

### SEVENTH DEGREE

The Lieutenant addresses the Knights: “I announce to you a <term>Master Mason</term> <note>The third and highest degree of basic 'Craft' or 'Blue Lodge' Masonry.</note> descending from Hiram <note>Hiram Abiff, the central allegorical figure of Masonic legend, said to be the architect of King Solomon's Temple.</note>, who asks to be received as a <term>Prussian Master Knight</term>; do you consent?” Immediately the Knights take their swords in hand, without saying a word, & present the points toward the body of the Candidate, who responds through the voice of the Knight Introducer, that he persists in the same sentiments, if it is the good pleasure of the Knight Commander-Lieutenant, & of all the Knights of the Lodge.

The Knight Commander-Lieutenant says, in the name of the whole Lodge: “My brave Knights & I consent, provided that you renounce all pride for the remainder of your life.” He answers: “I shall renounce it.” “Begin then by performing an act of humility.” Then the Knight Introducer, assisted by the first Knight in office, leads the <term>Candidate</term> <note>original: "Récipiendaire." The individual undergoing the initiation.</note> to the feet of the Knight Commander-Lieutenant, by three large genuflections <note>The act of bending the knee in worship or respect; here, it signifies the candidate's submission to the order’s values.</note> that he makes on the left knee; having arrived there, he prostrates himself before the Knight Commander-Lieutenant, who orders him to kiss the pommel of his sword;

# ON MASONRY.

solely responsible for the <term>Catechism</term> <note>The instructional script of questions and answers used to test a Mason's knowledge of their degree.</note>, may, for instruction, hold the <term>Table Lodge</term> <note>A ritualistic meeting centered around a meal, where specific toasts and rules of conduct apply.</note> for the <term>Fellows of Hiram</term> <note>original: "Compagnons d'Hiram". In this context, it refers to those who follow the tradition of Hiram Abiff, the legendary architect of Solomon’s Temple.</note>, at which nothing may be served that once had life; that is to say, it must be a frugal meal <note>This requirement for a meal without meat often symbolizes a return to the state of innocence before the biblical Flood or refers to the "Noachite" tradition of the degree, which looks back to the time of Noah.</note>.

Just as the Lodge is opened with three knocks, it is closed in the same manner; the first Knight in office responds with a single knock, and the Knight Commander-Lieutenant says to the first and second Knights in office: “Announce to all the Knights, present here, that the Lodge is darkened <note>original: "obscurcie". A ritual phrase indicating the formal end of the meeting.</note>, and that it is time to withdraw.” All the Knights, standing at the position of <term>order</term> <note>A specific ritual posture taken by Masons during ceremonies.</note>, say three times, in a mournful tone, *Phaleg* <note>original: "phaleg". In this degree, Phaleg (or Peleg) refers to the biblical figure during whose life the earth was divided and the Tower of Babel was built. It serves as the sacred word for this rank.</note>.

The gloves and the apron are yellow; the <term>Jewel</term> <note>The formal medal or insignia identifying the member's rank.</note> is an equilateral triangle crossed by an arrow, pointing downward; it must be made of gold, attached to a large black ribbon worn from the right shoulder to the left; unless it is worn at the buttonhole of the waistcoat, in which case it is made of silver.

# CATECHISM.

*Q.* Who are you?
*A.* Tell me who you are, and I will tell you who I am.
*Q.* Do you know the Children of Noah? <note>original: "Enfants de Noé"; in this degree, members identify as Noachites or descendants of Noah, predating the Solomonic tradition of Masonry.</note>
*A.* I know three of them.
*Q.* Who are they?
*A.* I will say it through the words S. C. J. <note>These initials represent Shem, Ham, and Japheth, the three sons of Noah according to the book of Genesis.</note>
*Q.* Give me the word.
*A.* Begin, and I will answer you. (*The three names S. C. J. are given.*)
*Q.* What do these words signify?
*A.* The initial letters form the sacred word.
*Q.* Give me the grip <term>attouchement; a specific physical touch or handshake used as a secret sign of recognition</term>.
*A.* Here it is.... by pressing three times, etc.
*Q.* Show me the sign. (*The sign is performed.*)
*A.* I shall satisfy you.
*Q.* Give me the password.
*A.* Phaleg. <note>original: "Phaleg"; also known as Peleg. In Masonic legend, he is often cited as the architect of the Tower of Babel and the progenitor of the Noachites.</note>
*Q.* Do you know the Grand Architect of the Tower of Babel?

<summary>This page presents the formal catechism for the Seventh Degree, utilizing a series of questions and answers regarding the "Children of Noah" and the architect Phaleg to verify a member's status.</summary>
<keywords>catechism, Children of Noah, Phaleg, Tower of Babel, S.C.J., Masonic ritual, Noachite, sacred word</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the catechism of the Seventh Degree, known as the Prussian Knight or Noachite. It moves from identifying the "Grand Architect" of the Tower of Babel to discussing the moral failures associated with that biblical event and the eventual repentance of its architect, Phaleg.</meta>

### SEVENTH DEGREE

*A.* Phaleg is his name.
*Q.* Who taught you his history?
*A.* The Knight Commander-Lieutenant of the Prussian Master Knights.
*Q.* In which Lodge?
*A.* In a Lodge where the Moon gave the light. <note>Noachite rituals were traditionally held at night, specifically during the full moon, to distinguish their "ancient" practices from the solar-aligned rituals of Solomonic Masonry.</note>
*Q.* Could one not have had another light?
*A.* No.
*Q.* Was this edifice <note>Referring to the Tower of Babel.</note> praiseworthy?
*A.* No, its perfection was impossible.
*Q.* Why was it impossible?
*A.* It is because pride was its first foundation.
*Q.* Is it to imitate the Children of Noah that you keep its memory?
*A.* No, it is to have their faults before our eyes. <note>In this degree, the Tower of Babel is presented as a cautionary tale against human arrogance and the desire to surpass God's will.</note>
*Q.* Where does the body of Phaleg rest?
*A.* In a tomb.
*Q.* Was he condemned? <note>original: "réprouvé"; meaning rejected by God or cast out.</note>
*A.* No, the agate stone says that God had pity on him, because he became humble. <note>According to Masonic legend, an agate stone was discovered in the ruins of the Tower (or in Prussia) inscribed with Hebrew characters detailing Phaleg's remorse and God's subsequent mercy.</note>
*Q.* How were you received as a Prussian Knight?
*A.* By the three genuflections <term>génuflexions; the act of kneeling on one knee as a sign of humility and submission</term>, after

<summary>This page concludes the catechism concerning Phaleg and the Tower of Babel, emphasizing that the degree serves as a moral lesson against pride and a reminder of the necessity of humility before God.</summary>
<keywords>Phaleg, Tower of Babel, pride, humility, agate stone, Prussian Knight, Noachite, moonlight Lodge, genuflections</keywords>

# ON FREEMASONRY.

having kissed the pommel of the sword of the Knight Commander-Lieutenant.

**Q.** Why did he have you perform genuflections <term>génuflexions; the act of kneeling on one knee as a sign of humility</term>?

**A.** To remind me that throughout my entire life I must practice humility.

**Q.** Why do the Knights wear a triangle?

**A.** In memory of the Temple of Phaleg <note>In this degree, Phaleg—the biblical figure during whose life the earth was divided—is reimagined as an architect who repented for the building of the Tower of Babel and built a triangular temple to God.</note>.

**Q.** Why the inverted arrow?

**A.** In memory of what happened at the Tower of Babel. The apron and the gloves are in memory of all the workers of the Tower. The black sash <note>original: "cordon"; a ritual ribbon or sash worn over the shoulder</note> is a sign of sadness.

**Q.** Do the workers work day and night?

**A.** Yes: by day, by the grace of the Sun's rays; and by night, by the grace of the Moon.

<image-desc>A substitution cipher key table featuring letters of the alphabet paired with geometric symbols. The symbols are variations of a Masonic or Pigpen cipher. The first row (a to l) uses simple grid-based shapes like corners and open squares, some with dots. The second row (m to z) uses double-lined versions (represented above with '=') of similar grid shapes for letters m through t, with different dot positions for q and f. The letters u, x, y, and z are represented by four directional angled brackets. Below the cipher is a decorative floral woodcut ornament featuring a central cross within symmetrical scrollwork and a small tassel at the bottom.</image-desc>

<note>This table provides the "Masonic Alphabet" or "Secret Cipher" specific to the Seventh Degree (Noachite or Prussian Knight). It is a variation of the Pigpen cipher, where letters are represented by the fragments of a grid. Such codes were used in the 18th century for sensitive correspondence between Masonic lodges and within ritual manuscripts to protect secrets from the uninitiated.</note>

# HISTORY
## OF THE NOACHITES,
-> O R <-
## PRUSSIAN KNIGHTS.

The descendants of Noah, despite the rainbow—which was the sign of reconciliation that the Lord had given to mankind, by which He assured them that He would no longer take vengeance upon them through a universal flood—resolved to build a Tower high enough to seek shelter from divine vengeance. For this purpose, they chose a plain named Shinar <note>original: "Sennar"; the biblical land in Mesopotamia where the Tower of Babel was built.</note>, in Asia; ten years after they had laid the foundations of this structure, the Lord (says Scripture) cast His eyes upon the earth, perceived the pride of the children of men, and descended upon the

<summary>This page begins the legendary history of the Noachite or Prussian Knight degree, detailing how Noah's descendants, fearing another flood despite God's promise, began building the Tower of Babel in Shinar.</summary>
<keywords>Noachites, Prussian Knights, Noah, Tower of Babel, Shinar, Great Flood, divine vengeance, Masonic history</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the narrative of the Tower of Babel from the previous page, explaining the origin of the name and the dispersal of the builders, focusing on the figure of Peleg.</meta>

HISTORY

earth to confound their reckless projects, and cast a confusion of languages among the workers; this is why this Tower is called Babel, which signifies *confusion*. Sometime later, Nimrod <note>original: "Nemrod"; traditionally considered a great hunter and the first powerful king on earth after the Flood.</note>, who was the first to establish distinctions among men, and who even avenged the rights and worship due to the Divinity, founded a City there, which for this reason was called Babylon, that is to say, enclosure of *confusion*. It was on the night of the full Moon of March that the Lord performed this wonder; it is in memory of this that the Noachite Knights hold their grand assembly every year during the full Moon of March. Their Assemblies of instruction are held every month, on the day of the full moon, and by the light of the Moon, as they cannot have any other light in the Lodge but the Moon. The workers, no longer understanding one another, were forced to separate, and each went his own way. Peleg <note>original: "Phaleg"; in the Bible, the earth was divided in his days, and in Masonic legend, he is often cast as the architect of the Tower of Babel.</note>, who had conceived the idea for this building and served as its director, was the most guilty; he condemned himself to a rigorous penance. He retired to the North of Germany, where he arrived after many hardships and fatigues endured in desert lands, where he found for—

<keywords>Tower of Babel, Nimrod, Babylon, Noachite Knights, Peleg, full moon rituals, confusion of languages, German history</keywords>

<header>OF THE PRUSSIAN KNIGHTS. 151</header>

any food, but roots and wild fruits.

In this region, which is called Prussia, he built several huts to shield himself from the harshness of the weather and the air, and a <term>triangle-shaped temple</term> <note>original: "Temple en forme de triangle"; in Noachite or Prussian Knight masonry, the triangle is a central symbol representing the Trinity and the divine order.</note>, where he enclosed himself to implore the mercy of God and the remission of his sin.

Among some ruins, while excavating <term>salt mines</term> in Prussia at a depth of fifteen cubits <note>A cubit is an ancient unit of measurement based on the length of the forearm, approximately 18 inches or 45 centimeters.</note> in the year 553, a triangular-shaped building was discovered. Inside, there was a column of white marble, upon the base of which the entire history was written in <term>Hebrew</term>. Beside this column, a sandstone tomb was found, in which was seen dust and an agate stone, upon which was the following epitaph:

> *Here rest the ashes of our <term>G. A.</term> <note>original: "G. A."; abbreviation for "Grand Architecte" or Grand Architect.</note> of the <term>Tower of Babel</term>; the Lord had pity on him, because he became humble.*

All these monuments are held by the King of Prussia. The epitaph does not state that <term>Peleg</term> <note>original: "Phaleg"; the biblical figure whose name means "division," traditionally blamed in Masonic legend for the prideful construction of the Tower of Babel.</note> was the Architect of the <term>Tower of Babel</term>; but the history which is upon the

<summary>This page describes Peleg's arrival in Prussia, his life of penance in a triangular temple, and the subsequent discovery of his tomb and historical records in a salt mine in 553 AD.</summary>

<image-desc>An emblem or "jewel" (the medal worn by members of the order) depicted as a triangle with a double border filled with stippling. Inside the triangle is a vertical central axis resembling a staff with a leaf at the top and a downward-pointing chevron at the bottom. A small circle with a central dot sits atop the triangle's apex.</image-desc>
